                    Session XL

                    Doc's Log

                    Doc’s Log: 1345 Hours, Saturday, June 12, 2010

                    Slept in til around 0900 Hours today. Considering how tense everyone was last night, I guess that’s probably a good thing. Meditation was one of those odd half full moments. It took forever, but when I finally got centered, I was really centered. Then I grabbed some food before working on the Emperor & Empress’s new clothes.

                    Karen came and got me before I could get very far. Apparently Nightshade contacted her daughter over Skype. I’ve used that one before. That’s George Jetson’s video phone made real on the Internet. Anyway, so Nightshade and Lil Nightshade talked for a while. Lil Nightshade seems to be suffering from the same forbidden visions her mother did, though it doesn’t sound like she is having the same backlash at home. That might be because she’s not telling her grandparents about what she’s seeing. Unsurprisingly, Lil Nightshade wants to come and see her Mom. Obviously that’s not possible right this moment, but I do think Nightshade the Greater should go to her. I’m not sure that Lil Nightshade should ever know about Dark Haven Bluffs, but that’s an issue for another time. Nightshade is handling it all very well right now. But oddly, Karen is not happy about it. I can’t tell exactly what it is that bothers her about the situation (there are many things to choose from), but something has really irritated her about this. Part of me wants to try and explain that opposing Lil Nightshade’s contact with the Greater will not be suffered well or long. Another part of me wants to just sit back and watch the fireworks. I am a terrible, terrible person.

                    But right now, I’m a terrible person that has to go and ritually burn some very expensive outfits.


                    Doc’s Log: 1500 Hours, Saturday, June 12, 2010

                    Wow. That was actually pretty cool. So in traditional or stereotypical, depending on how you look at it, it took longer to get the ballgown ready than the suit. Either way, they both look incredible in Twilight and I think they will serve their purpose excellently. And after our elated moment of bliss tonight, I’ll have some good quality ghostly finery. You know, for when we have to look our deathly best.


                    Doc’s Log: 2030 Hours, Saturday, June 12, 2010

                    Yeap I’m getting excited and nervous now. I’m not worrying about us using our powers for special effects. That’s is going to be really cool. I’m just...I think I might be the worst Gatekeeper ever. I realize the others are right that everyone that will show up is going to either believe already or think it’s fake anyway. But this just seems so wrong. My only comfort is that we will helping two ghosts pass on, one of which would have made a much bigger mess left to her own devices. So Bonnie Lynn gets her perfect date and Stanton gets his untouchable love. They both move on in what might be the most gothic love story backdrop ever and we get the rush of power from a job well done. Ronin in that cowboy hat and me with my new creepy rifle are also added perks.

                    Sadeeuk you burden yourself unnecessarily. Your personal duty to restrict Mortals and Specters from affecting the other is upheld. This gathering is of neither, but a confluence of both. You are affecting each, so that they have no need to target each other.

                    Why is it that he can make things like that so clear? Probably because I know he doesn’t have a social agenda or crave attention. Either way, it’s always appreciated when I can gain some perspective like that. Now I have to get my head in this and not make a fool of myself.


                    Doc’s Log: 2354 Hours, June 12, 2010

                    Okay, so I’ll try to get this all down while Stanton figures out how to put on his proper gentleman’s attire. We wanted a party that would get noticed. Holy Shit did we get one.

                    At 2100 Hours, we opened for business so to speak. I unlocked the Primeval Marionette and called as many crows as I could to the House. Then I moved to the second floor landing above the door to stare at people as they arrived and watch for vampires. I’d like to let any that show up know if they are the first so they will be prepared for any others and maybe we minimize the risk of … well anything. That I’m all Eric Draven-ed out and staring out the window with no light behind me so that really they just see a pale white face from an upper window is spooky and appropriate for the mood and theme.

                    Clarissa Walthstone and her inciting BFF Deanna showed up about 2200 Hours. We knew to keep an eye on them. Snowflake ascended the throne just after that so I went up next to be his Prince in Waiting. Ronin told us about a girl that was wandering the House seemingly tracing the Ley Lines. Rose thought she was an AMC student so she went to talk to her. Now this has never been a good idea in the past but there’s always a chance, right? Maria Smith, obviously an alias, was only so coy about what she was doing, but still didn’t give herself away. Ronin came up with Anders after getting a good look at her. Said the girl’s body had never died, but her soul looked like it was twenty to thirty times dead. Since we were getting all this intel through the ear curls, Rose just came out and asked her what she was. Well, one day she’ll learn to talk to people with a heartbeat.

                    At 2300 Hours on the nose, Stanley came in. Diedre and Tanya came in right before him and I decided that I should both meet our first special guests and finally introduce myself to Stanley. I went to Stanley first and he is a very charming old man. I can completely understand why Rose is so taken with him. As I let him roam and mingle, I moved to Diedre and Tanya. They seemed impressed with the party, though I think Tanya was a little creeped out. I suspect Diedre would have drug me off if we’d been alone. Two guys came in that Rose warned us about. Apparently RL and Jimbo were fond of a particular mode of dress that...well I just don’t know where it came from. The first thing I thought was that they were gay Prom King wannabes from the 60s. Apparently I wasn’t the only one as I noticed Stanley kind of appeared next to them and struck up a conversation. Eventually Maria Smith “rescued” the boys and Stanley returned to his happy roaming. I noticed next that Rose was going over to a jittery college girl who was keeping to herself. Now Rose is very pretty but she’s looks like a vampire at the best of times. In the House, given the atmosphere and purpose, she did not look alive. So when she walked up and started talking to Elsie, the poor girl almost spilled her drink she jumped so hard. Then I heard Rose tell her all about Bonnie Lynn through the curl. I’m not sure if Rose wanted to give Elsie a heart attack or not, but she was definitely feeding any fear spirits in the area. Snowflake decided he needed to talk to Nightshade briefly (without the ear curls) before taking the dance floor. King of the Ball was obviously his due, but there was something different in the way he looked at one of the girls. Tavia noticed too, but then he danced with her and whatever he did or said made her forget everyone else was in the House.

                    Stanley waved goodbye after only 45 minutes or so. I didn’t really see this as his scene but he made a good showing. Still before the witching hour, this disgusting hipster came in with a full 70s chest rug, silvered shades, and a very obvious blue tooth headset. Nightshade cleared him, well she said he could have taken advantage of her more in the past but didn’t, so no one threw him out. Apparently Don! the DJ is good publicity for Nightshade and the party in general. But that was about all the cue Snowflake needed. Suddenly the lights started flickering as a thick fog rose from the floor. I decided that though Don didn’t need to disappear, he needed to be aware I had my eye on him. Anytime he got near a window, the crows would gather. All would watch him, even as he moved. If he sat still, they would caw and peck at the glass. The longer he was in one place, the more birds would be in view of the window when he looked again. I figured this guy thought he’d seen it all, but I intended to make sure he’d never seen anything like this. As I moved through the crowd, I noticed Snowflake’s fog smelled of roses. I smirked to myself as no fog machine could do that. I walked by Maria Smith and heard one of the Prom Kings assure her in a shaky voice that when the ghost came, he’d protect her. I did my best Lurch impression when I glanced down and rumbled, “No you won’t.” I never looked back to see the look of horror on his face, but I did hear Maria giggle. Some tortured artist looking people came in with what I took to be a writer with an iPad. I assumed they were from the society section of the local papers. They looked pretentious, but I’m was dressed as Eric Draven, so you know.

                    Then Noriko came in. Rose went and gave her the polite, “Don’t Eat Anybody on the Grounds” talk. That went way better than I was afraid it might. But that’s when the trouble started. Zed and two other werewolves from Malard’s pack came in. I was pretty sure that Diedre didn’t ask permission to visit friends, but by the look on the woman’s face, she wasn’t here to talk. I moved to greet them quickly. I tried to talk to Zed since I knew him and we were battle friendly. Erin, the very tall woman, immediately took offense to me not addressing her first and got almost eye to eye with me (and in these boots that’s more than kinda scary). I thought I would try to defuse the issue when I told her that everyone was welcome here as it’s a party. As soon as I said it, I knew I’d done something wrong but I didn’t know what. Zed immediately started trying to talk Erin down in their language so I decided to move to greet my next set of special guests as Truman, Neon, and Rebecca had just walked in. Ronin noticed the tensions with Erin and moved down in time for vampiric introductions. I briefly explained to them my situation so they knew what was happening if something went wrong. Then I moved away to not inflame this Erin any further. Rose had moved closer to Deidre and Tanya to warn them. I passed the information through the ear curl of what happened. According to Deidre via Rose, I not only challenged their territory, but dictated what was permitted within it. Fabulous. The House fill of people, five werewolves, and three vampires and I’m about to start a turf war. A new vampire came in and the only things I know about her is that she is very hot and Ronin became her new best friend while they made introductions. It was almost midnight so I started looking for Stanton. I knew he wouldn’t be late for this. That was about the time Cupcake announced Trinh’s arrival to Nightshade. We’re the only three that know the whole story on that situation and with both Chandra and Karen on site, this was begging for a problem. And of course we got one. The next announcement was that Chandra was not in the security booth.

                    There have only been few times I’ve actually ever heard anger in Cupcake’s voice. This was one of them. So then we had an unknown variable vampire (I have to assume a member of the Establishment), 2 tense werewolves packs, a psychic teenager with the self-control of a ...well you know, and a rogue floating head that still hasn’t introduced herself to the vampire population at large. I’m not sure what happened, but based on the very short conversation through the curl, Cupcake was more than a little jealous. That’s a fight for a different night.

                    I found Stanton and took him up to get dressed. Wow...he just came out in his new fancy duds. I think Bonnie Lynn better be ready, because he’s gonna knock her socks off. And then all hell might break loose or we might just have a nice winding down of the party. Guess we’ll find out.

                    If I make it through this, I’m going to need to pay Malard a visit. I think an apology is owed. And while it might make it worse, I think it will mean more face to face.


                    Jasmine’s Diary

                    Saturday, June 12, 2010, Evening

                    Bonnie-Lynn’s ball is tonight. It starts officially at 10:00, and people can arrive as early as 9:00. I’ve spent most of the day helping to finalize our preparations: finishing the decorations, helping Alexia adorn her life-sized gingerbread men (which are really quite amazing!) and helping Bonnie-Lynn to get ready for her big night. She’s quite excited, but also very nervous about it, so she needed a bit of encouragement.

                    Everyone else has worked hard to get things ready as well. Tavia and Chandra are far too easily distracted, and I had to chastise them frequently to keep them on task, but I suppose they’re just excited. I do feel bad that Chandra cannot join us for the party and must remain hidden away in Gwen’s security booth, but it’s for the best, really, what with the other vampires that are certain to attend.


                    Saturday, June 12, 2010, Late Night

                    The party is in full swing, and seems to be going well, all things considered. I’ve retreated from the ballroom to help Bonnie-Lynn get dressed and ready for her grand entrance, and now we’re just waiting for the stroke of midnight. I’ve decided to take advantage of the brief respite to jot down a few of the things that have happened already during this eventful night.

                    I’ve been flitting about the ballroom, keeping an eye on the party-goers to make sure no one gets up to any mischief. Since we all decided to have a little fun with some “special effects” as it were, I’ve donned the Caul of Stillness so that I can move in and out of the shadows at will. It’s proven quite convenient for me to watch without being watched and to put others off balance by appearing suddenly from shadows they thought were unoccupied.

                    And the others are having an equally entertaining time, I’m sure. Doc seems to have engaged the assistance of the area’s crows, which have gathered around the house and lurk at the windows staring at those he wishes to spook. And Simon has been using his powers of illusion to create mysterious lighting and creepy fog that smells vaguely of old roses. There’s something achingly romantic about the atmosphere Simon’s illusions have created. It all seems so perfect!

                    Gwen has divided her time between her security room and the ballroom, and I’m glad to see she’s having a good time. Anders showed up, so she’s not alone. Actually, all of us seem to have a date here, except for me.

                    We haven’t really had much trouble with the normal people at the party. Yes, people have been making out in the corners and more-or-less openly doing drugs, all of which I find very uncomfortable, but as the others assured me, that’s only to be expected. Our supernatural visitors, however, have been cause for greater concern.

                    I noticed a rather pretty girl in glasses roaming about, examining the architecture, and I quickly determined that she was actually working out the feng shui of the house…or the ballroom at least. I suppose in western terms you would say she was looking for the ley lines or something. I recognized her from the house I visited when I was following Trinh once. Then, she’d been bossing R.L. and Jimbo, and Trinh around. And this time, since I was paying much more attention to her, I sensed something very strange: her soul has died twenty or thirty times, but her body has never died and she’s not possessing it. Very odd indeed. I mentioned her to the others through our ear pieces and then went to talk to her, hoping to find out more.

                    I could perhaps have been more subtle in our conversation, but I did make it apparent that I knew there was something mystical about her, and she didn’t deny it, but seemed equally perplexed as to what I was. Neither of us revealed anything concrete about our supernatural natures, but I did learn that her name is Maria Smith. She was pleasant enough, but obviously suspicious. I warned her that she shouldn’t investigate the house too closely, or it might take offense. It probably came off as a threat, but I only meant to keep her from doing anything that might endanger her.

                    While I spoke to her, Gwen moved closer and got a read on her with the Oracle. She could tell from Maria’s aura that she was curious, excited and suspicious. But she also said that Maria had one of the most vibrant auras she’s ever seen. Maria is certainly a magic-user. I just wish we knew more about her.

                    R.L. and Jimbo showed up eventually and met up with Maria. As far as I could tell, she tried to share her findings on the house with them, but they were paying more attention to some corseted goth girls than to Maria. Maria doesn’t seem to think much of them anyway, so I doubt she was surprised by their lack of attention.

                    Clarissa Walthstone (the psychic girl we helped before) came with her perky friend Deanna James. Clarissa hasn’t caused any trouble yet, and I doubt she will. I hope not, anyway. As long as she doesn’t become angry, we should be fine.

                    Elsie also attended with some of her friends. Since she was familiar with Bonnie-Lynn (having been haunted by her), I decided to approach her and tell her about what the evening had in store. I suppose I should have considered that her experiences with Bonnie-Lynn were a bit traumatic, because she became very nervous, even frightened, when I mentioned that Bonnie would be showing up later. I tried to encourage her, but I don’t think I was successful.

                    My friend Stanly showed up. The party wasn’t really his “scene,” but he decided to attend in order to show his support. He didn’t stay terribly long, but I was able to enjoy a nice chat with him. He was surprised to learn that I live in the Dark Haven Bluffs mansion.

                    Stanly did stay long enough for Mongler and Gretchen to show up. I made sure they were invited, after all, and was pleased to see them arrive as a couple. Stanly and Mongler are friends apparently, and gave each other a bit of a good-natured ribbing.

                    When Noriko entered, I was immediately on guard. Last time I saw her, she was hiding a body in the bathroom ceiling at Murder Ballads, and I didn’t want her murdering anyone at the party. Since I was responsible for keeping an eye out for potential problems, I approached her and we had a friendly, if tense chat, during which I tried to gain her assurances that she wasn’t going to be hunting at the party. She didn’t completely rule it out, but I got the sense that she wasn’t here for that, which probably means she doesn’t have a target in mind. I’ll have to keep an eye on her anyway, but I don’t think she’ll cause any trouble.

                    Deirdre and Tanya from the Sleeping Giant State Park werewolf pack showed up to enjoy the party. Doc pleasantly greeted them. I didn’t really speak to them directly, but Tanya looked well. It’s nice to see she’s adjusting to her new life. Unfortunately, some time later, several members of Mr. Malard’s pack arrived as well, including Zed and a woman I haven’t met before named Erin. They were aware that Deirdre and Tanya were there, and felt the other werewolves were trespassing on their territory. Doc tried to defuse the situation, but managed to make it a bit worse when he suggested that all were welcome at the house and party, which implied that he was overriding the werewolves’ territorial authority. I think Gwen and Alexia stepped in at that point to help out, and managed to keep things from devolving into violence. While the others were dealing with Erin, I went to Deirdre and Tanya to warn them that there might be trouble. Now we’re doing our best to keep Deirdre and Tanya away from the members of the other pack.

                    While Doc, Gwen and Alexia were trying to deal with the werewolves, Doc’s vampire friends Truman, Rebecca and Neon entered. Fortunately, they didn’t make any waves. I suppose they were there primarily to hunt, which we expected. But a bit later, another vampire entered. She was a sultry woman who immediately made me suspicious. Gwen went to find out more about who she was, but about that time I had to leave the ballroom to head upstairs and get Bonnie-Lynn ready for her grand appearance, so I’m not completely sure what Gwen discovered.

                    I guess the only other notable guests were an artsy-looking writer who seemed to be reporting on the party on his iPad, and a flamboyant-looking DJ who Natalie said was actually a fairly decent fellow. I didn’t speak to either, but I did notice Doc giving the DJ a tour of the party.

                    As I was leaving to help Bonnie-Lynn, I did notice Simon descend from his throne on the balcony overlooking the ballroom to dance with a few of the guests. He looked very genteel, and it was nice to see him get more directly involved in the party.

                    One other major crisis happened after I’d left to go help Bonnie-Lynn. Even though I had the ear piece, I wasn’t actually there, so I’m not completely sure what happened. Also, I think so of the others turned off their ear pieces while dealing with it. Essentially, from what I gather, Chandra disappeared from the security room and began roaming the ballroom after Trinh showed up at the party. She remained invisible, but she was just a floating, severed head at that point. Alexia tried to find out what Chandra was doing, and I think they had a bit of an argument over it. In the end, Alexia allowed Chandra to continue to roam the ballroom as long as she remained invisible, even though we had all decided that the chances of her being seen by someone with supernatural senses was too dangerous! And if she’s seen by the vampires, there could be catastrophic political ramifications, if what Doc and Dantaine say is true. Not to mention the possibility that one of the other vampires might react violently. Gwen was positively livid! And I’m not very happy about it either. Chandra is endangering a lot more than herself, and I hope she realizes that.

                    But for now, I’m going to put those misgivings aside and focus on Bonnie-Lynn. She is about to have the moment she’s longed for since she before she died, and we’re both very excited. I shall have to put my diary aside for now, though, as it’s almost midnight.


                    Footnotes

                    Hypocrisy

                    Natalie has been incredibly busy deejaying the party, and I gather from the crowd that she’s been doing a marvelous job. She’s even played a few songs that I’ve really enjoyed. There’s one band she’s played that reminds me very slightly of the Cure, and I’ve liked their songs. I’ll have to ask her about them later.

                    But I am appalled by the hypocrisy she continues to indulge in her treatment of other women! She’s always going on and on about her feminist ideals, complaining about the way some men treat women and the way some women, like Bonnie-Lynn, seem content to be defined by their romances. She seems to have this idea that being in love with someone is a form of ownership, and she’s always talking about empowering women. Which is all very fine, except that some of us actually see the mutual dependency of a healthy relationship to be a good and empowering thing.

                    But I’ve gone on about that before. The thing that’s really upsetting me right now is how she can get so upset about people objectifying women, and then she goes and unashamedly does exactly that! She’s actually got three scantily-clad girls (I believe the American term is “skanks”) up in her deejay booth with her, hanging off of her like they’re her property or something. And they’re only there to make her look better and to stroke her ego. She’s showing them off like jewelry, and if that’s not objectifying women, then I don’t know what is! It seems rather indulgent and misogynistic to me. The girls themselves don’t seem to care, but it boggles my mind how Natalie can complain about Bonnie-Lynn’s need for romance and love and not take issue with these “groupies” and their obvious need to find self-worth by attaching themselves to a local celebrity. Were they behaving in the same way toward someone else, particularly a man, I’m sure we’d hear an earful about it.

                    Isolation

                    Despite the distractions offered by all of our interesting guests, I have had ample opportunity to observe the party attendees and to reflect on my own place in this party. I’ve never really been to any parties like this before. All the parties I’ve ever been to have been formal affairs connected somehow to my career as a professional swimmer. Growing up, I was only rarely invited to “fun” parties. In fact, I only attended one or two, and even then only because my Kiwi cousins took me when I was visiting my mother’s family in New Zealand.

                    The problem with me and parties is that I can’t relax and just…have fun. So there’s always this sense of separation between me and everyone else at the party, like I don’t really belong there. I think the English term is “wallflower,” but I’m not sure if what I’m feeling is exactly the same thing. It’s not just shyness. It’s a feeling that there’s an uncrossable chasm separating me from everyone else there, who seem to so effortlessly lose themselves in the enjoyment of moment.

                    At our party, I’ve separated myself on purpose so that I can monitor the guests and keep them from doing anything unfortunate. But I wish I could give in and become a part of the party instead of just watching it like an outsider. Nothing deepens one’s loneliness like a crowd in which one does not really belong.

                    I’m hoping that I will be able to relax and enjoy the party more once Bonnie-Lynn and Stanton have made their appearance. Perhaps the romance of that fated encounter will help bridge the chasm that distances me from everyone else tonight.

                    Simon's Musings

                    Saturday, 2010 June 12

                    I wanted to feel what living was like again. With but a few hours
                    left to our event, I needed to spend it gathering, and summarily
                    casting out, my thoughts. Resting in the mausoleum, listening to the
                    cassette of overlaid moans, screams, and scratches, my best efforts to
                    reproduce the background soundtrack to my living hell. Surrounded by
                    the colors of my vomit and blood and the tortured words of my young
                    mind, I go back to that place. And with the echoing sound of the tape
                    decks click, everything goes silent, and I return – I return stronger,
                    just as I did then.

                    However, I was not met silently by the Prince. Perching, as he always
                    does, this time upon the future grave of my cousin Tavia, he turned
                    his head to the side and grew a disconcerting smile before asking,
                    “What is wrong, my child?”

                    I tried to explain my fears. My krewe knows me as I am inside the
                    house, as I was before I died, and that innocence I am trying to
                    recover. My death was transformative – it offered me the freedom to
                    make choices. A freedom I am not fit to have. In my chest pocket, I
                    have an old photo of my father Norman from the 1970s, clad in that
                    corduroy suit, dancing with some woman. I needed it for the ritual to
                    allow me to dance with his grace. There is so much I could have
                    learned from him, but as blood flew from his head, it felt so right,
                    so I swung again. And again. I will never know so much because of
                    that moment. “Or will you know more than he could have taught you?”
                    the Prince rebutted before returning us to the subject, “What are you
                    afraid of?”

                    I couldn't have them see me as I was outside. All I did was use
                    people, scare them, manipulate them, all for fun. This party was to
                    be that scene, and I'm scared. It's the implosion of worlds. My last
                    adventure out, the changes here had taken hold. I never used to
                    connect with outsiders. But I did. I felt emotions – I identified
                    them – and I acted on them. There was too much going on tonight. I
                    had to stand by my krewe.

                    “Yes, but you're looking too many moves ahead in the wrong chess game.”

                    Then he started asking me what I remembered of people I'd interacted
                    with. The person I spoke to the most during my club trip in February
                    – she had purple hair, her card was the queen of cups (breast jokes
                    followed), she stayed at our table and Julian flirted with her all
                    night. I didn't recall her name. Then he asked about Andrea. She's
                    19, going to Brown, studying religion, her favorite color is a cobalt
                    shade of blue, her parents were hippies, father an academic, mother a
                    writer – I kept going. I listened to her talk all night, so surely I
                    knew things about her.

                    “You're afraid of being human.”

                    I argued with him. What sense did that accusation make? I'm human,
                    except in the ways I'm not. I shouldn't have stormed off at that
                    point. I should have listened to him. Things might have been easier.

                    The Prince spent the early part of the party sitting in the throne
                    next to me, silently judging and shaking his head. When Andrea
                    wandered in, his sinister smirk returned. “You can't hide from her,
                    Simon. It'd be a shame if you saved her from a poor suitor to have
                    her stabbed by Tavia. Are you more human or demon now? Have fun
                    finding out, ye who doesn't want my help.” My heart skipped a beat as
                    Tavia greeted her at the door.

                    I danced with Elsie first. She was scared, and I thought comforting
                    her might calm me down. It didn't. The Prince stood at the edge of
                    the light tapping a pocket watch.

                    I danced with Andrea second. I thought that might make the choice
                    look more random, but I could not control myself – I felt an
                    indomitable compulsion to impress her. For a moment, the room was
                    gone, the crowd was gone, the werewolves and vampires, the ghosts and
                    spirits, all gone. As the song wore on, I found myself back in this
                    sordid existence, none of my problems resolved. I tried to stammer
                    some of my usual drivel, partially honest this time at least, about
                    the danger of spirits, but it was no use. I didn't want her to go.
                    And by that point, I'm sure Tavia noticed us.

                    So, I danced with Tavia. I tried to disarm her, but I tried
                    desperately to avoid those few words I knew would work. I wouldn't
                    say that I loved her and I wouldn't speak of our wedding. I don't
                    think she left reassured. I couldn't muster the enthusiasm to dance
                    as I had before, because with her I feel trapped in the shadows of the
                    past. With her I feel that nothing is possible, that all is decided –
                    I feel like as dead as when I was alive. All the while, the Prince
                    galloped around us with an invisible partner, with taunting glances.
                    At one point he swept close to us, whispering “The truth will set you
                    free, Simon.”

                    After I formally thanked Tavia for the dance, I stood for a moment
                    amidst the dance floor in utter panic. The Prince stood at my side,
                    and we had an exchange that changed my attitude towards the evening.

                    “Will you listen to me now?”
                    “Yes.”

                    “Do you know what you want?”
                    “I'm not sure.”

                    “Do you know what to do about your tryst, the werewolves, the
                    vampires, or, well, anything?”
                    “No.”

                    “Then pretend like you do, and take charge.”

                    I put my ear curl in, resolute. I don't know what I was thinking, but
                    I was going to take action.

                    End Session XL


                    Well that was fun wasn’t it? Too much fun to cram into one session, clearly.

                    If you forgot Elsie was the student who was haunted by Bonnie Lynne. R.L. and Jimbo were Teddy Boys that Jasmine has run into a few times, and Maria was something of an instigator who Jasmine encountered dealing with R.L., Jimbo, Trinh, and Pilot Light. Clarissa was the teenage telekinetic, and Deanna her friend. Trinh is at the center of a lot of mess, helped her grandmother move on and she is being stalked by Chandra and was at the center of a brief torrid affair with Natalie (in which she dealt with her bisexuality very badly). Truman and co. are Carthians Doc had as contacts, and Deirdre was the werewolf who was friends with Alexia and had a one night stand with Doc…Tanya was the girl the Krewe helped when she began turning into a Werewolf herself. Zed they saw once at Malard’s and he helped them against the Ivory Claws. That is a chunk of the randomly dropped names I think.

                    Part of the goal was, of course, to re-introduce old characters who had briefly crossed the stage, and introduce new ones. I think it worked. But also to sit back and see how the PCs wanted to handle a big party like this. Good practice, ideally, for flesh fairs to come.

                    But it was all very much good times. Fun to see the PCs running around managing a party of such scale. If anyone has any questions or wants clarification let me know. And hey, if you are exposed to new music you find you like, bonus!

                        Session XLI

                        Doc’s Log

                        Okay this is even late for me, but I’m still pretty keyed up from the party and the ceremony and everything so I wanted to get this written before I start forgetting the cool parts.

                        Rose and I stood at opposite staircases just before midnight with our charges at the ready. Stanton was a little nervous but he kind of forgot everything when he saw Bonnie Lynn. I have to admit with what Rose did with her hair and the dress, she was very pretty. Then Nightshade made the announcement that “our visitors from beyond the grave have joined us.” Snowflake made the mists rise and I cued the crows about the time Stanton materialized and Bonnie Lynn appeared in the mirrors lining their staircase. Rose and I escorted them to the bottom of the stairs and then they moved together and started to dance. The goths all made a hole...actually they mostly just cleared the floor in awe. Nightshade played a slow song, something fitting for their time, not this one.

                        I watched the couple for a minute. It was one of those moments of purity and rightness. I don’t know, maybe the kind of thing that gives people hope. Here were two people that died in gruesome ways, denied their true feelings by society, time, and even Death. Yet here they were, alone in a crowded room, enjoying in death what they could not in life. I looked around the ballroom. Except for the pretentious artist twits, the reactions were generally those of quiet amazement. Fuck the movies. This is how things should be.

                        Glancing around, I noticed Rose had moved over to Trinh. It came out that she was still having what she called “weak spells” and I knew that would be trouble later. I sent word through Rose that Trinh should stop by the shop sometime. I knew I didn’t have anything to stop her problem, given Cupcake would be upset if I killed Chandra and cut out her liver. But I did have some things that would boost her system a little and that would at least help. Then Nightshade made the fair well announcements so Rose and I escorted our ghostly guests out to the garden. We figured they could continue their special night alone.

                        When I came back in, Snowflake “projected” himself out over the rail and down to the dance floor. Don came over to Snowflake for what looked like an interview, but when Snowflake swung his hand through Don to show he wasn’t there, the DJ decided there were other places to be. Next Snowflake walked over to Zed and the rest of his pack. Then we learned through the ear curl that he has apologized and things seem better. I’m still going to talk to Malard tomorrow...er later today. I should probably take Snowflake and cookies with me, but we’ll see about that part. Then as if remembering that the werewolves were more interested in Spirits than humans, Rose possessed the Drown Bride to double check how things looked in Twilight. She gave the all clear and then almost mischievously prodded Ronin about doing the same for the party. This would have been awesome but then we all remembered that only ghosts and Sin-Eaters would be able to see it. That was kinda disappointing. Snowflake was making the rounds by then and walked up just as I was about to start talking with Truman and crew again. We talked a bit just to relax and keep up appearances. They pointed out that it was so good that they didn’t have anything to do with all these Masquerade Breaches. I assumed there would be issues for them using their powers as blatantly as we had. Snowflake and I agreed that was really good that they had nothing to do with these things.

                        Then Ronin said through the curl that Noriko was stalking something but she couldn’t tell what. I decided that it was my turn to babysit for a while. Thinking I was witty, I grabbed one of the cords off the drapes on the way into the room. I asked what she was doing somewhat playfully to keep things friendly and I was flipping the cord around like a string. She immediately started looking from me to the string. I succeeded in grabbing her attention, but I did a better job than I intended. Before I realized what was happening, Noriko literally pounced me. I went from talking to her to having her pressed hard against me; we were kissing and she was running her hands through my hair. I can’t lie and say that was a terrible thing, but I kinda came to so to speak when her shirt started coming off. That immediately shocked me back to reality. I backed off and tried very carefully to talk her down without pissing her off. I’ve dealt with jilted women a few times but never a jilted werecat. It didn’t help that Ronin was groaning like my kid sister and talking about how I needed to stop doing that over the curl. That embarrassed me even more and I wasn’t sure how Nightshade might react. I certainly didn’t plan to wind up with another woman attached to me tonight. Nightshade thought this was hilarious and was trying to egg me on. Noriko took it better than I was afraid she might. She called me a tease when I apologized for sending the wrong signals. I promised her that if I ever tried to “play with string” again, that I’d be serious or deal with the consequences. Then my phone rang as Nightshade was giving me a chance to escape. Using the call as an excuse, I told Noriko they needed me upstairs. She accepted that and we went our separate ways.

                        How in the hell can I be damn near invisible to women almost my whole life, then I find all these supernatural beauties. First Deirdre, then Nightshade, now Noriko. I’m not stupid enough to suggest it, but I’d almost believe Nightshade would consider all four of us in bed at once. When I was 18, that was a dream come true. I’m old enough to know better now. Nice thought though it might be. And besides, I want to prove to Nightshade that I can be there for her without all this weirdness. I am SO glad Nightshade is not the jealous kind. It was kinda embarrassing (moreso) when she asked Ronin to save a copy of the whole thing with Noriko for her. Ronin thought she meant for blackmail, but she freely admitted that would be for “me time” later. That helped so much. Probably blushed through my face paint.

                        When I finally got everyone to shut up about seducing a werecat, Snowflake asked me to come meet him at the left staircase. I walked back through and noticed RL dancing with Dierdre. He looked terrified and she looked tremendously amused. I really didn’t know what to think about that so instead of having another Forrest Gump moment, I kept moving. I met up with Snowflake at the stairs and noticed two others heading our way. I didn’t recognize the guy but the girl was the same one he danced with before Tavia. Snowflake asked me to “distract” Tavia for about 5 minutes. It didn’t take a genius to realize what was happening, so I quietly agreed to be the distraction. I’ve had worse requests than being Snowflake’s wingman. On my way over to Tavia, Truman stopped me. He wanted to let me know that the local Sheriff was dropping by. I didn’t see any cops but he looked at me a little harder and I realized he didn’t mean mortal authorities. He carefully pointed out an Asian man subtly studying people as he moved through. Truman let me know that they were going into hiding and then headed off. I took a closer look at him as I continued toward Tavia and alerted the others. He was definitely undead but he had somehow turned on multiple body systems. That’ll be a talk for Truman and I to have later, I guess. The way this Sheriff was studying people reminded me of Ronin doing aura perceptions. I kept track of where he was and moved on to Tavia and Karen. We talked for a while and keeping Tavia’s attention during an interesting conversation is never hard. I noticed Snowflake’s friend asked Rose to dance. She had been looking somewhat mopy again and I suspected Ronin had something to do with it. But she accepted the offer. Surprisingly, they looked good together. She might have even enjoyed herself. I decided to ask Tavia to dance as well. I’d given Snowflake more than his needed 5 minutes, but there was no reason to rush off. We twirled around the dance floor and I definitely did not look like a clumsy moron this time.

                        Once we finished dancing, I wandered upstairs for a few songs. Then about 0245 Hours, I asked Nightshade if we could dance to one of her songs. She was a little unsure but I gave her a heads up before the party so she had a few songs loaded up. On the way downstairs together, I asked Snowflake through the curl if I could get that spotlight we talked about. As Starlit Nocturne played, a pale spotlight shone upon us seemingly from nowhere as mist encircled the edges of the light. Once again I didn’t make a fool of myself or of Nightshade, but I didn’t think I really wow’ed her either. Admittedly getting caught making out with a werecat might have done a little bit reduce the wow factor.

                        About 0300 Hours, Zed and the rest of his pack left without incident. Rose came over and told me Nightshade and I looked lovely on the floor. It was bittersweet knowing that I impressed the romantic but didn’t get a reaction from the woman I danced with. I thanked her and mentioned she didn’t look too bad with that mysterious stranger I caught her dancing with earlier. She admitted she was having a good time and I told everyone through the curl to mark the date. She shot me a look but it wasn’t nearly as damning as others I’ve seen. About thirty minutes later the party started to wind down a bit. Ronin reported Chandra had returned to the security room. Best we could tell, it worked out to be roughly right after Trinh left. That in turn left Cupcake looking down, which we weren’t sure was possible, so we cheered her up a bit.

                        Nightshade made the announcement that the party would be shutting down in 15 minutes at 0345 Hours. People begrudgingly started getting their stuff together. We noticed Clarissa and Deanna were some of the last to move along. Snowflake and I caught up to them and learned that neither one was sober enough to be driving. Snowflake talked them into staying for coffee. I’m hoping we can manage longer but I’m not sure how we can ninja them back into one house or the other in broad daylight. Don came and thanked everyone on his way out. He was still concerned as he walked out. The crows hopped around until he had a path to his car but was still surrounded as they closed in behind him. I’m a terrible person because I thought that was hilarious. When I looked back from the window, I noticed that Rose got one last dance with the mystery man. No one mentioned Jean and that’s okay.

                        Once everyone left that was leaving, we setup for the ritual in the backyard. At sunrise, Rose led the Pass On ceremony, but this time it was more of a wedding style ritual instead of Ronin’s funeral style. It worked well and both ghosts faded away like ocean mists, holding each other as the light brightened the horizon. Rose actually smiled and for that one moment she looked as happy as anyone I’ve ever seen. The hues of dawn even cast some color on her cheeks. With her happy and Cupcake sad, it was all very surreal.

                        Now I’m going to crash. Looked like Karen was going to stay with Nightshade so don’t think I’ll be much but in the way with that. I’ll see how bad of an idea going to see Malard sounds like when I wake up.


                        Jasmine’s Diary

                        Sunday, June 13, 2010, Early Morning

                        I’ve just returned to my room to sleep, and I am exhausted, but there is so much I need to write down before I drift into the realm of dreams. Bonnie-Lynn’s ball is over, and it was magical! In the end, it lived up to everything I had hoped!

                        When the clock struck midnight, it was time for Bonnie-Lynn and Stanton to make their grand entrances. So Doc and I escorted them to opposite landings on the grand staircase that leads onto the ballroom floor. We presented them to the awe-struck crowd as Natalie introduced them. Stanton was able to manifest, but he was unmistakably a ghost. And Bonnie-Lynn appeared only as a reflection in the numerous mirrors we had placed everywhere. Both of them were stunning, and quite frankly I can’t blame Bonnie for being smitten with Stanton.

                        Bonnie and Stanton marched elegantly down the staircase, and when they met, Stanton took Bonnie’s arm and escorted her onto the dance floor. The other guests backed away, leaving a circle around the ghostly couple as they danced to the graceful waltz Natalie played. Everyone at the party simply watched them dance in reverential silence, compelled by the enchanting presence of two love-struck ghosts.

                        It was one of the most beautiful things I’ve ever witnessed.

                        The Bride and I chatted warmly with one another as we watched from the shadows. The sense of triumph we felt was almost euphoric, and I was glad she was there to enjoy the moment with me.

                        The dance seemed to last a blissful eternity, yet it was over too soon. Doc and I returned to Bonnie and Stanton and escorted them out of the ballroom and into the gardens behind the house, so that they could enjoy each other’s company in private, accompanied only by the sweet night air and the pale moonlight.

                        The crowd was awed and mystified. They expected the ghosts to be some sort of sham, but almost all of them knew that they were watching the real thing. Even Don!, Natalie’s DJ acquaintance, was impressed, and I gather that he made a big deal of broadcasting the fact that there were real ghosts at the party.

                        After Bonnie and Stanton left for the gardens, the rest of us got back to the business of keeping things under control. I happened to notice my kitten, Fu playing with Noriko when she strayed close to the stairs. Xerxes was keeping a wary eye on my cat. He’s a very protective dog. But I decided it wasn’t safe for Fu in the ballroom, and after thanking Noriko for looking out for him, I took him back upstairs to my room.

                        Speaking of Noriko, we caught her stalking people at the party, and I offered to go and make sure all was well, but Doc decided to take care of it instead. Unfortunately, it wasn’t really his night for dealing with were-creatures, and he did something to excite her passions. She tried very hard to become amorous with him, much to his chagrin. Fortunately, he was able to disentangle himself before she had her way, but I gather that Natalie didn’t care one way or the other if he fooled around with Noriko. In fact, she said she wanted the video of the two of them making out! I honestly don’t know what’s wrong with her! It’s as though letting Doc fool around excuses her own promiscuity, or even worse, turns her on! I was disgusted, and removed my earpiece so I wouldn’t have to listen to the rest of that discussion.

                        That said, in retrospect I’m not really being fair to Natalie. As far as I know, she has been faithful to Doc since they became “official,” so I can’t criticize her for any sort of infidelity, aside from Karen’s presence in their relationship.

                        Well, I don’t want to dwell on it.

                        I noticed that Simon was having fun “projecting” his soul with his illusions, making a show of passing through people. It did a lot to reinforce the reality of our ghosts. I think he also helped to smooth things over with the werewolves, which was quite a relief. He spent awhile chatting with Truman’s group of vampires as well. I got the impression that the vampires were concerned by the blatant supernatural displays at the party, so I do hope Simon put them at ease.

                        As I meandered through the ballroom, I ran across Trinh. It was a pleasure to see her again, and we chatted for awhile. She mentioned that she felt like something was watching her at the party, but I assured her that the house simply had that effect on people sometimes. She’s still been suffering weak spells, which is troubling, as we had hoped they were connected to her grandmother’s ghost. The fact that she’s still suffering those spells now that Vong’s ghost has passed on is troubling. Otherwise, though, she seems to be doing well. She pointed out a young man named Steven (who has the odd nickname “Pilot Light”), who has been a friend of hers since childhood. He was completely drunk, but Trinh says he’s a really decent guy who has always looked out for her. I wonder if there’s any romantic potential there. I gave her a hug and asked her to give my regard to her parents before I moved on.

                        I visited Alexia after that. She was sulking in a corner, angrily munching on a piece of one of her life-sized gingerbread men. I asked her what was wrong, and she told me about Chandra disappearing and their argument. And it was during that discussion that I figured out that Chandra has been feeding on Trinh, which explains the weak spells and Trinh’s sense of being watched.

                        I was not happy. In fact, I was quite angry to know that Chandra might be endangering my student.

                        Alexia says she told Chandra to stop feeding on Trinh, which drove a bit of wedge between them. Chandra is strongly drawn to Trinh for some reason, and I fear she might transform Trinh into a penanggalan. But I don’t think Trinh has suffered a new weak spell since Alexia insisted that Chandra stop feeding from her, so I hope that Chandra truly has respected Alexia’s wishes. Still, Alexia’s unhappy that her girlfriend is unhappy, and Chandra’s behavior is dangerous. I promised to help Alexia talk to Chandra about it and get her view of thing in the next day or two. I don’t like seeing Alexia unhappy. It seems unnatural. But Chandra is my friend as well, and I can’t let her do anything we’ll all regret, especially since it involves an innocent girl.

                        I was still conversing with Alexia, commenting on how much I wish I could relax and enjoy myself at the party instead of reaming so distant and aloof from it all, when a fierce-look young Asian man in a leather jacket came stalking into the party and began looking about as though searching for someone. I was immediately on guard, and it didn’t take long before Doc informed us that our latest guest was the vampire “sheriff,” which I gather is some sort of policeman among their kind. Gwen thought he was probably using supernatural perception to study people, so I cloaked myself in the shroud of invisibility and began to shadow him. Fortunately, he didn’t notice me despite whatever supernatural sense he has.

                        He didn’t really cause any trouble, though. And Gwen became annoyed with me for disappearing. I told her I hadn’t really been doing anything else and wouldn’t be missed, but she insisted that she’d seen several guys eyeing me throughout the night. She said they probably hadn’t asked me to dance because I kept disappearing. We argued a bit, but she finally convinced me to reappear. And almost as soon as I did, I was approached by a handsome young man named Julian. I suspect that Gwen engineered it all somehow. But she meant well, and honestly, I’m glad she did it.

                        Julian turned out to be quite charming. He was a wonderful dancer. I discovered that he is a friend of Simon’s from Providence. He’s a graduate student and a poet, so we talked a bit about the romantics. I almost ruined everything, though, when he tried to transition from talking about Simon to talking about me, and I clammed up. I think I recovered in time, though I must have looked the fool when I became tongue-tied and said I was originally from Thailand (not Taiwan) before correcting myself. Still, I must have made a good impression, because Julian asked me to dance with him again for the night’s last dance.

                        I liked him a lot. He was romantic and genteel, and seemed to know just what to say and do to put me at ease. I confess that I wouldn’t mind seeing him again. The Bride was quite smitten and has been encouraging me not to pass up this opportunity. Fortunately, Simon can put Julian and I in touch with one another.

                        I wasn’t the only one who had a nice dance toward the end of the party. Doc also managed to get Natalie to leave her DJ booth and her groupies for a dance. She and Doc looked so romantic and happy together.

                        I think that dancing with Julian put me in exactly the right romantic frame of mind for helping Bonnie and Stanton pass on once the party was over. After the guests had been cleared out, we all went to the garden to find the ghostly lovers. They were sitting close together on a stone bench, hands clasped and love in their eyes. We told them it was time, if they wanted, for them to be joined in holy matrimony. Bonnie was happier than I’ve ever seen her, and Stanton was practically glowing.

                        I conducted a simple wedding ceremony for them in the half-light of the approaching dawn. At its conclusion, once they said their vows, Bonnie and Stanton faded away as they kissed. The Drowned Bride and I shared a rare and pure joy, and it must have showed because the others seemed happy to see me smiling. Not the sad smile that occasionally brushes my lips, but a real, heartfelt smile that managed to find its way to my eyes as well.

                        And my soul is still smiling. But the tides of my sorrow are beginning to rise again despite the Bride’s attempts to stem their flow, and I can feel the tears beginning to well up as my happiness gives way to longing. I want to be in love again so badly I can hardly bear it. And I miss Richard so very, very much.


                        Footnotes

                        Mourning and Celebration

                        After Bonnie and Stanton had retired to the garden, Gwen and I somehow got into a conversation about mourning and celebration. I’m what we sin-eaters call a Mourner. Despite my hopes to find love again, I am more comfortable with ghosts than I am with the living. Death is not an ending. Not really. It’s just a change. A sad and mournful one to be sure, but also a necessary one if one is to fulfill one’s karma. And it’s my duty to help ghosts come to peace with their deaths so that they can pass on into the karmic cycle again.

                        And yet, every time I resolve another ghost, I grieve, even as I celebrate. For me, mourning is a form of celebration in which one remembers those who have moved on. It’s a time to be glad that they have found peace, but also a time to regret their absence. In doing so, we cherish the person who is no longer there and burn their memory into our souls so that they will, in a sense, always be with us. For despite the pain of grief that comes with mourning, it would be a far greater tragedy to forget those who have passed on. Wouldn’t it?

                        Gwen didn’t understand when I told her I thought of mourning as a celebration. To her, celebration is joyous and boisterous, not a somber reflection on loss. She gave me the look that says, “Honey, you need to loosen up and get shagged.” She gives me that look a lot. I’m not annoyed by it, though. It’s simply her way of sharing her concern for me.

                        Even if we don’t quite understand each other, I’m glad to have a friend like Gwen.

                        End Session XLI

                        The waltz the ghosts danced to is not listed, we did not specifically name it.

                        Let’s see…The oddities with the ghosts were as Stanton could Manifest while Bonnie Lynn was more a see in mirrors kind of ghost. What he was actually thinking was unknown (no one could get an empathy roll off on him). I intended Don! to be a little enigmatic, I think it worked.

Apparently Pilot Light had a terrifying evening.

                        Last night’s session was the next day and a half dealing mostly with drama from the party. Apparently some of the PCs are Very Dramatic. I hope you will see us then.
