                                            #56
                                            11-22-2013, 06:01 AM
                                            Hello folks. This session’s journals for Doc and Jasmine cover this session and the one two weeks before. Natalie’s tweets just covers the last session. That’s about it so I will let them do the talking. If you want to read the original Candle Cove Creepypasta they are discussing, it is Here

                                            Session XLVI

                                            Doc’s Log

                                            Doc’s Log: 2125 Hours, Sunday, July 18, 2010

                                            Nothing new on the Nightshade front. I’ve gone on patrol several nights and been back to train with Sebastian a few others. I’m trying not to worry or stress over it too much. Apparently I’m not hiding this well. Sebastian told me that I was not focusing pretty much as soon as we started Wednesday night.

                                            Karen doesn’t really hang around the rest of us so it’s been harder for me to talk to her without making it noticeable that that’s what I’m doing. Given the conversation with Chandra about not turning Trinh, I don’t really want to make that a public announcement. Not that I have the slightest fucking idea of how to go about this conversation with Karen either. “Hey so, the woman we’re both seeing wants to have a baby with me. Thought you should know. Oh yeah, and do you have any ideas as to what’s going on?” Karen is a little awkward around me sometimes as it is, I’m sure that will be the most comfortable conversation ever.

                                            In more horrific news, Ronin came in this afternoon before dinner and spoke to Tavia. That in itself was a little scary but Tavia mentioned that while shopping for another Snoopy toothbrush, she heard some kids talking about a TV show that she recognized. It was called Candle Cove. At the mere mention of the name, a wall in my mind started to crack. I started to remember. The two different little girls named Janice, the pirate Skin-Taker, the longer cape after the first Janice “left”, and the screaming. Oh God, the screaming! I felt my blood run cold. The dead marionette eyes staring while the jaw slid back and forth. The feeling of dread when I was five.

                                            I was broken from my traumatic trip down Memory Lane when Snowflake pointed out that he and Tavia always enjoyed the show when they were little. Tavia’s brother never liked it though, which raised a few flags in my mind. First, I’m alot older than Snowflake and Tavia and that show went off the air after the dreaded second season. So how the hell did they ever see it? Second, Tavia’s brother was actually her half brother. The only real distinction there is the Outsider connection Tavia shares with Snowflake. Cupcake said something about the show when she was a kid and she’s older still, so I’m not even sure how any of this is possible.

                                            We talked about how maybe the television station could be haunted, but Nightshade checked the Internet and said the station moved way back when I was like still a little kid, so if that was the case, the ghost moved too. Just as we were discussing other possibilities, Snowflake started humming that damned calliope music and Nightshade mentioned something about a radio signal. All that together combined in my head to form possibly the most horrifying sci-fi nightmare I’ve ever imagined. Back in the ‘60s, we shot radio and TV signals into space as part of the Space Race. The thought was that they could send examples of human life for friendly Extra Terrestrials to see and want to come visit. But the people that did that didn’t know about the other realms out there. That’s what I needed to think about. Sleeping will be fun tonight.

                                            Now I’m headed for the library with sketchpad in hand. I don’t know I’ll get any more research done on our supernatural neighbors and their sociopolitical climate, but I know I can draw in peace and try to think. Well, unless I find Gretchen eating dust again. Then I’ll go on patrol.


                                            Doc’s Log: 1015 Hours, Monday, July 19, 2010

                                            Meditation ran long again. Was cutting it close getting to the shop in time for open. I’m sure it’s because I don’t know exactly what’s up with Nightshade. That still doesn’t help the matter. And the “John Carpenter’s The Thing meets Michael Bay’s...well anything” nightmare likely also made it more difficult.

                                            Work was pretty smooth for a Monday morning. I rotated some of the perishables, dusted, and then started drawing some possible designs to get familiar with them. Just really simple things to start with since I’m still getting use to the whole drawing thing. I’m not worried about the gloves or the equipment, those things I’m used to dealing with and keeping clean. I’m just concerned with the art. If I’m going do to this by appointment, I need to make it worth my time and the customer’s.


                                            Doc’s Log: 1830 Hours, Monday, July 19, 2010

                                            At lunch, Nightshade came around and as usual brightened up my day. Then she told me about a plan she and Snowflake hatched to head over to the local TV station and try to get more information and maybe a copy of the Candle Cove show. They know I need to be around to run the shop but this was serious. With Rose still in Taiwan and Ronin out researching another case, I really didn’t think letting them walk into a possible hostile zone was a great idea. So after lunch, Cupcake, Snowflake, Nightshade, and I all went to the WTNH News 8 station.

                                            When I was a kid they called it Action News, then they switched the name to News Channel when I was in high school. It was a big scandal for the old folks. I think I was a sophomore because I remember getting my license and hearing people bitching about it on the radio on the way to school in the mornings after Christmas.

                                            This whole Candle Cove thing has me thinking alot about the times before my death. I’ll try to reminisce about the old days later. Anyway, Nightshade had a plan to get us in the station without too many questions. She told the folks up front she was working on a parody of old shows and needed to see several. This would give us more time. Given her reputation as a new wave Elvira, no one really questioned the matter. It took us a while to find the reels we needed because all the stuff from when they moved (something like 20 years ago if I remember right) was stuck in a closet stacked up on a table. Then we had to sort through all that to find the actual Candle Cove reels. Cupcake found them and we pulled them out into the main room where Nightshade had her laptop and video equipment. Before we tried to do too much, Cupcake object read the reels. They haven’t been shown since 1974. I wasn’t born in 1974! Cupcake sketched out a picture of “person” that brought the reels in originally. She was a very thin woman that stood about 6’5” with nearly white blonde hair. But as she continued to draw, that was just an illusion. She, it, was actually a skeleton in a long white coat that moved like a marionette. This thing was visible outside of Twilight. We also learned that it was the same one that filmed the show. This thing has to be at least Geist level powerful, possibly even moreso. Cupcake also sketched out the lady that accepted the reels for the station back in the day. She was apparently very nervous around the skeleton like there was something off, but she didn’t see through the illusion.

                                            I’m glad I was only there to serve as body guard. My mind was reeling from all the memories and the truth of the matter. I had that same feeling of dread I felt while the final episode was airing. I figured out pretty quickly after I died that I had always been marked by Death in some way. That explained why I could see how people would die growing up. But this whole thing started before I was born and has affected so many children throughout all of New Haven since. I couldn’t shake the feeling that I was one of the lucky ones. I turned the TV off. What about all those kids that didn’t? And even more terrifying to me, what if this was what started it all for me? What if this was all engineered somehow so that I would end up a Sin-Eater?

                                            I came back to acute focus as Nightshade threaded the reels and prepared to copy the old film into a digital format. Oddly, when it started none of us could see anything. There was only static. Snowflake looked over his shoulder and looked confused. He said the Little Prince could see the show. I possessed the Dog but still didn’t see anything more than static. When Snowflake did the same, he informed us that he could see while looking through the Little Prince’s eyes. The Little Prince also agreed with us that there was something odd about the show. I had a terrible thought that maybe this was being rebroadcast from beyond. I mean we knew these originals had not been used since before I was born and Cupcake might have seen the original airing but we’re not sure. I voiced that fear as I couldn’t think of anyone else that I could talk to about it. Nightshade mentioned dead signals worked sort of like that. Broadcasts that only transmit within the Underworld. If something like that can happen, who’s to say that this transmission isn’t coming BACK from space? Or worse yet...from Outside?

                                            Snowflake changed the subject to consider Boneyarding around schools to try and learn when the show is airing now. Then Nightshade started talking about sex at school. I know she actually enjoys the idea of sex in a church, but apparently not in a school. Still there were ideas that happily took over my attention for a few minutes to drown out the mounting terror rising in my mind. Just at the end of that conversation, a very pretty, twentysomething woman walked in. She introduced herself as Becky Franklin, an employee of the station. She heard that Nightshade was in the building and wanted to come and meet her. Cupcake and Nightshade were both suddenly VERY attentive. All I could do was smile. Becky quickly proved to be just as intelligent as she was cute. She seemed to be aware of Candle Cove and had researched it a bit on her own. Nightshade immediately became very flirtatious and pumped her for information. Becky also claimed to be able to hear something while Nightshade was copying the reels, but that might have just been her excitement talking. As it happened, Becky knew where to find records for the people involved with Candle Cove at the station. Snowflake volunteered to stay behind with the equipment while the rest went with our new associate. I knew he would be watching us with Boneyard so this was amusing to me.

                                            In the records room, we learned that the woman Cupcake sketched was Evelyn Peters. Becky told us that Evelyn hanged herself sometime back. There was no real investigation even though there were strange circumstances. The family didn’t like to talk about it either. I made a mental note to have Ronin look up obituaries and try to get any other public record on Ms. Peters later. The station manager at the time was a man named Amos Wright. Becky said that he was very well loved at the station and there were still people around that thought highly of him. He lives in New York now and we found his number. By that point, Nightshade decided that Becky was useful enough to keep in contact so email was exchanged. I just shook my head as we headed back to the storage closet. When we got back, Snowflake was bopping around to thankfully unheard calliope music.

                                            Given that we’d spent most of the day at the station, we left after Nightshade had copied both seasons of the show. Since we didn’t have Rose around to be displeased with bad choices, and I’m unlikely to get bitchy about food considering how much we work it off, we ordered pizza and chocolate pie for dinner.


                                            Doc’s Log: 0315 Hours, Tuesday, July 20, 2010

                                            After dinner I walked into Karen grilling Nightshade about being bad. I’m not entirely sure what they were arguing about, although I suspected it had something to do with Becky. Given that I was there with both of them the whole time, I knew nothing had happened. And I got vaguely annoyed that Karen didn’t trust Nightshade (or me either I guess) any more than that. I knew it would go badly if I got involved so I went down into the Library for a while. While I was down there, I think I just started fuming because my annoyance intensified. I called a cab and snuck out. I’m still not completely sure I knew what I was doing, but I sent to a fabric shop and bought a ten foot strip of silk print. It was black with a red pattern in the center. Given that my intention was to use this to tie Nightshade up, the symbolism was heavy.

                                            I got back in time to find Chandra floating around as a head in front of Karen. I know this probably isn’t the first time and Karen certainly didn’t seem phased by it, but something about that whole situation seemed wrong. Possibly more disturbing was that Chandra said she could hear something on the digital recordings of Candle Cove Nightshade was working on. Not well enough to really make anything out, but between the Little Prince and Chandra they determined that she was correct.

                                            After that, we started watching Twin Peaks as a group on Netflix. The surreal hits just keep on coming. After a few hours of that, I headed out to go on patrol. I knew I needed to get away for a while.


                                            Doc’s Log: 1430 Hours, Tuesday, July 20, 2010

                                            It’s been an interesting day. I got up a little earlier than normal, but my mind was not at peace. It took two hours to meditate this morning. I really didn’t have time to really even speak to the others, just run out the door. I’ve gotten a group of folks that have taken to coming in on Tuesdays and they all came in throughout the morning so I was helping customers, staying busy, and not really able to draw. I can’t complain though. With all the strangeness of my shop hours lately, I’m just glad I have some loyal customers left.
                                            Nightshade came over for lunch and we talked alot. Not really about my concerns, but the concerns of the Krewe. Apparently, they believe I need an employee. Now I have considered that, since I will get more income from the tattoo and massage appointments, but other than Leslie de la Poer, no one was immediately coming to mind. In talking with Nightshade, Elise, Trinh, Clarissa, and Deanna all hit the list. Though to be honest, I don’t want Deanna around as she’d spent most of the day trying to figure out how to mix the right things together to get high. Clarissa has the added problem of destroying the shop if someone pisses her off. Having two that could work might be good considering Leslie, Elise, and Trinh are all in college so will have class schedules to deal with. The high school girls are really not even an option. Elsie has less issues from the start, given Trinh is still targeted by Chandra and I know she has an uncomfortable history with Nightshade. All three have some knowledge of the occult, though Elsie is again less likely to get involved, given a choice anyway. Definitely something to consider. I wonder if I could bring in enough for all three.

                                            After lunch, things stayed slow so I went back to drawing. I’ve been working on some protective symbols. I think some of my special clients might be looking for those. Thinking about it, Leslie might need some of those.

                                            Nightshade just sent me a text saying the others were successful in figuring out when the show comes on TV again. Also sounds like Cupcake was almost arrested before they figured it out too. I’m not sure I want to know what that’s about.


                                            Doc’s Log: 0115 Hours, Wednesday, July 21, 2010

                                            Karen and Cupcake took Snowflake out to spy on the schools. Apparently this involved Cupcake driving Snowflake’s 1970s ride very slowly around playgrounds. It’s a miracle they weren’t arrested. They even had Snowflake in the trunk so that was very special too. Eventually they switched out to Karen’s car and managed to not get spotted as easily. This is good as we have to pick Rose up later today and having to explain to her that one of the Krewe was arrested for creeping around playgrounds would not have been a conversation in which I wanted to be involved. But since it was Game Night and we were still Rose-less, we enjoyed ourselves with faux debauchery. We played cards with booze and monopoly money. I won a few rounds, avoided excess drinking in general but then lost the last hand. By then, Tavia had past out, Karen and Chandra were frisky, and that pretty much ended the night. Karen and Chandra were dragged off and I carried Tavia up to her room. Snowflake and I just sort of looked around at the silence in the house and sighed. I left him to his thoughts and came to my room. I don’t think I’m ready for sleep yet, but just to be on the safe side, I’m staying in tonight.



                                            *******************

                                            Session 46

                                            ___________


                                            Doc’s Log: 1145 Hours, Wednesday, July 21, 2010

                                            Apparently I’ve needed some extra sleep or more alcohol. I woke up on time and got ready for my meditation. Given how hard it’s been lately, I was really surprised when I was centered before I realized it. I feel completely at peace.

                                            I came to work after breakfast with the Krewe. They are going to pick Rose up from the airport today so that’s going to be happy. As difficult as she can be sometimes, it’s been weird not having Rose around for the last two weeks. Actually she stayed over an extra two days, but still. The important thing is that she’s coming back. I’m sure we’re going to be very busy for a while now. I think I’ll draw until Nightshade gets here for lunch.


                                            Doc’s Log: 1645 Hours, Wednesday, July 21, 2010

                                            Nightshade came by for lunch and told me Rose is home already but is sleeping off the jet lag. Probably a good call really. I noticed Nightshade seemed a little tired but she did drink more than just when she lost. Still, there was more involved there. But it was not for me to worry about. Today, here in the shop, she was my smiling Strawberry Deathcake. I might have been a little more affectionate than usual but she didn’t seem to mind. It’s the moments we have together like these that remind me just how happy we can be together.

                                            After she left, I started working on another drawing. This one was more detailed and less likely to be a tattoo. It was difficult because I’m still not used to drawing faces. I decided to try and actually draw Nightshade as Strawberry Deathcake. If I can ever get good at this, maybe I’ll draw a comic of it. A gothic femme fatale that leads a group of wayward women back to their own sense of gothic purpose and self confidence. I have no idea. I’m just enjoying the challenge and I suspect Nightshade would really like it if I can finish. But now I should probably get the shop ready to close up. I’m sure everyone is going to want to hear all about Rose’s trip.


                                            Doc’s Log: 0350 Hours, Thursday, July 22, 2010

                                            Well tonight was certainly what you could call exciting.

                                            When I got back to the House, Rose was just getting around again. She brought quite a bit of presents back with her and “Stuff (TM)” for the House. She was obviously very happy to be back too. That’s a major change for Rose as she’s rarely very happy about anything. Over dinner, we caught her and Ronin up on all the Candle Cove developments. It was a little strange because half the Krewe had never heard of this show before a few days ago, while the other half had pretty effectively blocked it from memory. There was lots of discussion about trying to jam the signal. Ronin found the obituary for Evelyn Peters fairly quickly. There wasn’t much there, but then she pulled up the police records on it. Her friend Officer Tart had worked that case. She’s going to talk to him when she can to see what he remembers. The main plan we’ve put together so far is to plant a device in the TV station that will broadcast over the right frequency. If it works like Nightshade and I think, then this would block the Candle Cove signal. Ronin and Nightshade are putting together the device and programming to make this happen. Snowflake and I are working on the distraction piece of our plan. See there’s no way we can get into the station and hook this up while the employees are still there. I don’t remember who thought a bomb threat was a good idea, but we stuck with that to clear out the people. Even with getting everyone out, if we plant our device with a bomb threat it will be found. So Snowflake decided that the illusions should become a bit more real. We determined we could make a fake bomb for the authorities to find. They could pretty quickly figure out that it was a hoax and everything go back to normal. So I got a two pair of surgical gloves so nothing we bought could have fingerprints and then Snowflake and I went shopping. I knew the metal box and wires we could get at Radio Shack. The Play-Doh, alarm clock, and newspaper were from wherever we thought of first in different areas.

                                            When we got back, Ronin was coming in as well. She called us all together to fill us in. Tart, the believer in everything, was one of the officers that worked the investigation of Evelyn Peters. From the reports gathered at the scene, Evelyn’s grandchildren were watching television. She became irrationally angry and forced everyone to leave the home. Tart had several details that were not included in the reports. He said the children claimed to be watching Candle Cove when Evelyn entered the room and she became hysterical. She had apparently told her own children never to let the grandchildren watch that show. Of course, we now know the adults would have no way of determining this, but apparently Evelyn could see it. After telling everyone to get out, Evelyn locked herself in the house. Tart said they found her hanging in front of the television, facing the screen. He also noted that there was a strange feeling of being watched in the room with the television. But strangely no one would turn it off. He asked another officer about it and was told that it wasn’t necessary to turn it off. Tart compared the overall weird feeling to the way he felt while searching for children in the Patch.

                                            After that, Rose needed to talk to us before we went back to our various other projects. While she was in Taiwan, she learned that her uncle had disappeared. She said the family was not really fond of him as he was odd. Her description of Sung Guang seemed more scientific but her family thought he either was or planned to molest her as a child. However what Rose told was a very different story. He provided her with a place to discuss her visions of death without stigma unlike most of her family. She couldn’t say if he was only interested in that or not, but it was still an outlet for her as a child. She of course investigated his home while still in Taiwan. She noted an odd sensation in the house but could not determine from what. There was also evidence of an occult ritual which took place before he vanished, but she was unaware of its purpose or origin. Lastly, and most importantly for me, she also found an Avernian Gate nearby. She asked, once we were less pressed for time in other problems, if the Krewe could help her learn the truth. I think it was fair to say I spoke for the Krewe when I told her we could help her find out about her uncle. But when I said I’d also pop over for proper take out once she taught me the language, she actually smiled. It was a real smile, filled with Hope; something she rarely does.

                                            After that we split off into our working groups to finish preparing for our station raid. Snowflake and I were stumbling through what I thought would be a simple design in the Library when we heard a door slam and then a loud crash. We met the others as we ran upstairs. All the Krewe and companions were accounted for. It was the room with the weapons rack and the normal sarcophagus. When we opened the door, we could weapons and the Egyptian coffin stuck into the wall and door...as it hurled with great force. I checked with Snowflake, but this room wasn’t one of the trap rooms. We couldn’t find any visible reason so Snowflake checked for any hidden people. The House was clear, but it was apparent something had been there. Cupcake checked one of the weapons sticking out of the door for any psychic impressions and we hit the jackpot. An intruder was lured into the room and attacked. It had a male voice when it screamed as the blades and sarcophagus flew towards him. Ronin ran back to the control room and checked the cameras. Fu was playing in the hallway when he looked up and walked into a room as if looking for something. Then the door to that room closed. A blurry image appeared in the hallway above the kitchen. That put him dangerously close to our rooms. Strangely all the doors to our rooms closed and locked before he got to them. As we watched the video, we could see how the House was herding this uninvited guest down each hall until finally he came to this room. Then the door to that room slammed and we all heard the crash. I unlocked my Stigmata Shroud and stepped into Twilight.

                                            I ran to the window and scanned the withered landscape. All I could find were a few ghost cars on the road and Marguerite trying to get home again. It was a long shot as I estimated our stranger had about a 25 minute head start. I suspected a vampire because of the blurry video image, but I had no idea of any that might be able to step into Twilight. That alone was disconcerting. Though the fact that we were all being protected by the Fish God below was oddly comforting; at least to everyone but Ronin. She was greatly insulted that someone had sidestepped her security in such a way. We walked back to the room and Cupcake object read the floor where Snowflake pointed. She was able to give a slightly better description. He had yellow eyes, greyish green skin and his fingernails were very long. We couldn’t get a good look at his face because he wore a hood with a cloth over his face. Almost like he expected and prepared for something like object reading. The only other details the floor could tell us were about his slippers and that he was average weight for his size. Once we got all that, we put the sarcophagus and weapons back in their original locations. Snowflake and I noted we’d probably need to repair the walls since we wouldn’t have the supplies to do so until tomorrow, but that was the least we could do in thanks to the House.

                                            I called Truman to see what he might know of such a creature as we discovered. I think they were watching a movie based on the background noise. Truman strongly disputed that a vampire could do something like that. Though he was very interested in trying to create a bloodline that he called the Teleportessi, which could do just that. That sounded like the late night, goofy ramblings of a college dorm, but I didn’t mention it. We finished up and I thanked him as always. I heard Neon ask before Truman hung up if he was high. I took that to be a good sign we needed to go back to our bomb making. We finally finished it in such a way as to be believable a little before 0200 Hours. After that, I left for a short night of patrols.

                                            Now I’m going to bed.

                                            Jasmine’s Diary

                                            Wednesday, July 21, 2010, Evening

                                            I’m back in New Haven, and it’s late at night. I’d probably be dead asleep right now if I hadn’t slept off some of the jet lag this afternoon; I think I slept almost 6 hours.

                                            Gwen, and Alexia picked me up at the airport. I was really happy to see them again, and I got the feeling I was missed while I was away. It’s nice to feel that you’re a part of something like the krewe. Coming back from Taiwan, where I’ve always been a bit of an outsider even in my own family (my father and mother excepted, of course), I was reminded of how much of a family the krewe is. And it made me feel good.

                                            Gwen and Alexia updated me on some things the krewe has been dealing with over the past couple of days, but they waited until dinner so that the whole krewe could discuss all the details and get everyone caught up. Gwen’s been away on a new case, so to some degree, she needed filling in as well. So over dinner we had a long talk about all the horrible new things that have suddenly cropped up.

                                            I’ll try to summarize as best I can, but there’s a lot of information, and I’m not as familiar with it as most of the others. Essentially, we’ve discovered evidence of a locally broadcast show called Candle Cove, which local children have been talking about lately. Doc, Tavia, Simon, Alexia, and Gwen all remember seeing the same show when they were growing up.

                                            The show itself sounds absolutely ghastly. It involved several mismatched pirate puppets and a little girl named Janice who got into adventures in a place called Candle Cove. The villain in the show was a terrifying marionette called the Skintaker that would frequently stare at the audience and say “I’m going to take your skin!” while grinding its teeth back and forth. Everyone of us who had seen it remembered it being terrifying, and also seemed to recall that only children could see the show; adults saw only staticy noise on the screen while it was showing.

                                            The show ran two seasons, but the girl who played Janet changed at some point, and after that happened, the Skintaker’s cape had grown longer. Doc and the others have a very strong, if vague suspicion that the original Janet had died and the Skintaker had taken her skin to add to his cape.

                                            The last episode of the show was apparently quite terrifying, with everyone screaming while the Skintaker ground his teeth and horrid, discordant calliope music played in the background. All the krewe members who saw the show felt a sense of dread and turned it off before the episode ended, but stories of missing children suggest that not all children were so wise, and that something terrible might have happened to those who watched the episode to its unpleasant end.

                                            Quite frankly, I’m glad I didn’t see the show when I was a child. What’s odd is that the show seems to be a constant thing, appearing to most children in New Haven when they are around the age of five or six. That’s when Doc and the others saw it, even though they are all very different ages.

                                            One other disturbing detail is that Tavia and Simon weren’t bothered by the show, and in fact liked it, even though Tavia’s late brother reacted just like everyone else. That certainly suggests that this show carries the taint of the Outsiders; after all, Tavia and Simon have Outsider blood. Of course, they are also a bit mad, which could also explain it.

                                            Candle Cove is currently airing at 4:00 every week day, and at the rate the episodes seem to be playing, we only have a couple of weeks before the show reaches its final episode. So we have to stop it before then.

                                            The others have already started on this. To gain access to the TV station’s old footage, Natalie, Doc and Alexia made up a story about needing old tapes to film a parody show like Elvira, whatever that is. They managed to dig up the show’s original tapes, and Alexia used the Industrial key to unlock her oracular abilities, and learned that the tapes haven’t been shown since 1974, which makes their continued broadcast even more mysterious. She also got a glimpse of the woman who delivered them to the TV station, and sketched a ghoulish image of a tall, thin woman with a skull face and long, snow white hair and a white coat. She says the woman moved like a marionette, but her appearance was hidden by an illusion.

                                            She also learned that a woman named Evelyn Peters received delivery of the tapes. Gwen’s going to investigate her tomorrow.

                                            Doc and the others also learned that the station manager when the tapes were originally shown was a man named Amos Wright. He’s now retired and living in New York, but contacting him might not be a bad idea as well.

                                            Anyway, Doc and the others viewed the tapes at the station, but saw only static. Simon’s geist, the Little Prince, could see the show plainly, though, as could Simon when he possessed his geist. Also, they later discovered that Chandra can apparently hear or see vague sounds or images on the tapes, but nothing clearly, likely thanks to her heightened vampiric senses.

                                            I don’t know if its significant or not, but the krewe also met a young reporter named Becky Franklin while investigating the tape. Becky’s been researching the Candle Cove phenomenon for some time, and was able to fill in some of the details. I got the impression that Alexia and Natalie were altogether too taken with Becky.

                                            I’m a bit…put off by my krewe-mates’ plans in regard to the Candle Cove investigation. Granted, I can’t really think of anything better, but I would prefer something a little less…unethical. First off, the day before I returned from Taiwan, Alexia, and Karen decided to stalk the schools, with Simon using the Boneyard to eavesdrop on the schoolchildren and see if they were talking about the show. Naturally, the police accosted them and they never really learned anything useful.

                                            And now, they’re planning on infiltrating a local radio station to secretly commandeer one of their unused broadcast signals in order to create a signal that should, hopefully, jam the frequency on which Candle Cove is being broadcast. It may only be a temporary solution to buy us more time to resolve the situation, but the idea itself is quite sound, and I approve. What I don’t approve of is the method the others settled on to gain access to the station machinery. Simon and Doc and building a fake bomb, which they intend to plant in the station and then phone in a bomb threat to force everyone to leave the place! I can hardly believe we’ve even considered such a plan! I disapprove.

                                            Fortunately, I’ll have no part of at, as I’ll be investigating another matter entirely. Time is running out for us on resolving the ghosts of Sansbury Hall since students will be returning in less than a month, so I’m going to get started on that. We had a bit of a revelation. We know that one of Alonia DeCastro’s anchors is her amulet, and that the other is Adolphe’s corpse. We’ve been fools not to realize before now that his corpse must be somewhere in Sansbury Hall; otherwise, Alonia wouldn’t be haunting the place! We just need to find it. If we can do that, we can resolve Alonia.

                                            Unfortunately, we still don’t have Adolphe pistols, and it doesn’t look like Ildefonso is going to be able to produce them. So the best we can do now is removing Adolphe from Sansbury Hall by letting Simon establish his own dominance over the building, thus destroying its value as an anchor for Adolphe. Then he will be solely bound to his pistols, wherever they are. Hopefully we’ll be able to track them down soon.

                                            We also discussed moving Marjorie into Nilan Hall now that Bonnie-Lynn is truly gone. It would be a good way to keep the haunt there active, though I do feel a bit bad that we’ve moved her around so much. The poor ghost is confused enough as it is.


                                            Wednesday, July 21, 2010, Night

                                            We’ve mostly been involved in preparing for our different plans since dinner. The bomb threat and signal jamming attempt at the radio station is going to happen tomorrow, so Doc and Simon spent the day constructing their fake bomb, while Natalie and Gwen have been working on software to generate the signal.

                                            Meanwhile, Alexia and I have been working on translating all the Taiwanese recipes I got from my great-aunt. All the recipes are in Taiwanese, and while I don’t have any trouble translating most of the text from Chinese to English, I don’t actually know what some of the ingredients are in English, so Alexia’s help in sorting that out has been invaluable. I’ve enjoyed working on it with her, and it’s nice to see her becoming excited over all these new culinary possibilities.

                                            Gwen also had a chat with her police friends, and learned that Evelyn Peters committed suicide some years ago, though really we suspect that Candle Cove claimed her life somehow. Apparently she caught her grandchildren watching Candle Cove, and in an angry fit drove her family from the house. When they returned, she’d hanged herself in front of the TV. And the police, while investigating, felt no desire to turn off the television. Very odd.


                                            Wednesday, July 21, 2010, Late Night

                                            We’ve had an intruder! Someone appeared inside the house and began looking about. It’s not clear from the camera footage whether he meant to be here or not, as he seemed somewhat lost. But he was unable to investigate much in the house, because the will of the house itself channeled the trespasser into a certain room by locking all doors except those it wanted him to open. The room it led him to was full of weapons. As soon as the trespasser entered the room, the door slammed shut and the weapons flew from their racks to impale the intruder, but he teleported away just in time to avoid death. It was the slamming door that alerted us to something amiss, but the intruder was long gone before we arrived. I suspected at first that the trespasser might have been Maria attempting to further satisfy her curiosity over the house and its ley lines, but it wasn’t.

                                            Thanks to Alexia’s oracular abilities, we were able to learn that the intruder was a figure wearing a hooded cloak, and that he was a ghoulish thing with cold, grayish skin, long, yellowed nails and teeth, and yellow eyes. We have no idea what it might be. It’s not a werewolf, and it seems unlikely that it’s a vampire. According to Doc (who called his friend Truman to confirm the details), vampires can’t teleport. While they might seem to do so by vanishing from sight, they are still present, just invisible, and in this case a vampire would have been slain by all the weapons. It’s conceivable that it might have been a changeling, so I’ll ask Mongler about it tomorrow. Until then, we really have no leads.


                                            Footnotes

                                            Doc is a Sweetheart

                                            Over dinner, I told the krewe about the mystery of my uncle’s disappearance, and asked that they help me find out what happened to him once the more pressing matters of Candle Cove and Sansbury Hall have been resolved. They were all very willing to help out. And the Doc surprised me by asking if I’d found any Avernian gates while I was in Taipei. I had, of course, and so he said we could head there whenever we liked through the Underworld. He has a ritual, one I’ve considered learning myself, that will let us travel there quickly. So getting back to Taiwan to investigate what happened to my uncle will be far easier that I thought

                                            It brightened my day, and I can’t express how grateful I am to Doc for giving me a little bit more hope. He even offered to use the ritual to pop in to Taipei for some authentic take-out from time to time after we’ve figured out what’s happened to Uncle Guang. Of course, he had one condition: I have to teach him to speak Mandarin. Which of course I’d be happy to do.

                                            I hope Natalie realizes how lucky she is to have someone like Doc.

                                            American Culture and Idioms

                                            Angel

                                            Gwen says that I will absolutely adore a show she likes called Angel. It’s not on the air anymore, but that hardly matters with her collection of DVDs and Netflix. Apparently it has a lot to do with vampires; she referenced it when we were discussing vampires and the fact that they don’t actually have all the weaknesses the fictions say they do, like being unable to enter a home without being invited in first. I don’t really think I would like a show about vampires, but Gwen seems convinced, so maybe I’ll give it try.

                                            Home Security

                                            I think most of us were happy to see with our own eyes how the house protects it own. I have severe reservations about the violence with which it did so, but at the same time, the camera footage showed that it even made an effort to protect my kitten, Fu, by luring him safely away from the intruder.

                                            Gwen was not happy, though. She was upset that her security system had not detected the intruder, or perhaps that the intruder had foiled her system by simply teleporting in. This led to a discussion of motion sensors and other additional security measures that the rest of us decided were really too much.

                                            I made the mistake of saying that Gwen’s system probably just couldn’t be expected to handle some of the supernatural threats we are likely to face. Honestly, with my Caul and Shroud of Stillness, I could bypass it without too much trouble. But Gwen did not want to hear that. The system is her child, after all, and not to be insulted.
