                                                11-22-2013, 06:51 PM
                                                Hello folks, it’s time for some journals. Just Doc and Jasmine today, though Natalie should have one next time unless something unexpected happens. As a note, some of the content this time may be a little triggery for some people, so just bear that in mind.

                                                Session XLVII

                                                Doc’s Log

                                                Doc’s Log: 1120 Hours, Thursday, July 22, 2010

                                                Bomb-making is hard work, least when you have no real idea what you’re doing. Meditation took a bit this morning, but nothing unnecessary. I was able to eat breakfast with the Krewe and still get to the shop without any trouble. It’s been a pretty normal day. I like that. Hopefully it’ll stay that way.

                                                I think I’m going to start doing a little more planning for my drawings. Title a few that I plan to use as tattoos, organize the more elaborate images, and try to coordinate the Strawberry Deathcake ideas with things I can understand clearly how to draw. That should keep me busy for a while.


                                                Doc’s Log: 1410 Hours, Thursday, July 22, 2010

                                                Nightshade came over for lunch today. There’s an unspoken question hanging in the air but I’m trying to keep everything positive. Driving her away now is really not the best idea I’ve had lately. I am going to have to corner Karen and soon. When she left, I went back to drawing to help clear my head.

                                                A little later, Nightshade called me on a conference line so the Krewe could discuss new information. Rose spoke to Mongler about Changelings and our intruder. According to him, Changelings have to use doors. The weird part of that is that they have to use doors to travel between realms, similar to how we use Avernian Gates, or at least that’s how I understood it. Monger did say that he thought it might have been wizards. The dinosaur man says they are around and they use big magics. I don’t know if he means wizards like the kind of magic Snowflake and I use, or if there’s something else out there that we haven’t found yet. Knowing what Snowflake and I have uncovered and future talents we could develop, I don’t want to think about any “big magics” that we might have to deal with.

                                                In other news, Gretchen has officially crossed a boundary from crazy chick in the Library into Occult Researcher with Secrets of the Beyond. The good part of this is she knows it’s not good for anyone else to deal with and wants nothing to do with involving other people. That means we don’t need to kill her at least. Mongler is apparently doing well with dating Gretchen...so much so that she doesn’t want him knowing about her work to protect him. She’s unfortunately told him enough about it to make him want to keep her safe from whatever terrible thing she always talks about killing her. I talked to her about these things a while back before she and Mongler were together. By the sound of things, she’s found significantly more now and expects to pay for her transgressions with her life and more. Given that she’s been researching what (not who) originated the de la Por branch of the family, this puts Outsiders high on the list of possibilities. With that thought in mind, I have to wonder if it’s ever going to be safe for Gretchen to leave Dark Haven Bluffs in any sort of permanent way? Anyway, this all came up because Mongler told Rose how worried he was. Rose, being very concerned over this budding relationship, spoke to Gretchen about things. I won’t lie, I can only imagine how awkward and uncomfortable that whole conversation was. Rose didn’t take well to the notion that Mongler should just be okay with Gretchen suddenly disappearing so her eternal soul could be tormented, or whatever Gretchen thinks is going to happen to her. We pretty casually committed our intention to find, stop, and somehow remove whatever might be coming. Snowflake entered the conversation about then and, even though I wasn’t there, I could just see the look of cautious confusion on his face. He wanted to know, given his actual ancestry, where this put him. I immediately answered that he was not part of the problem. It’s weird how he really does seem like my little brother. I don’t know for sure, but I think sometimes he’s afraid we’ll forget that he isn’t something from Outside. It’s times like that that I really want to try and explain to him what a real family is like and how our Krewe is one. Either way, we are pretty sure that Gretchen is on the trail of Outsiders. And that means it becomes of interest to us.

                                                More immediately pressing, the Triangulation experiment is at 1600 Hours today after Snowflake, Rose, and Cupcake do some more elaborate recon of Sainsbury Hall. They are planning to wear the ear curls so we can all keep up with what’s going on. I think someday we should record one of these little outings for something like an old style radio horror. I should probably talk to Nightshade and Ronin about it.


                                                Doc’s Log: 1550 Hours, Thursday, July 22, 2010

                                                Well, this was a damned eventful afternoon.

                                                I was walking around the shop with my ear curl in, which kinda bothered a few of my more paranoid customers, so I could keep tabs on what the others were up to. It was a little tense to be honest. I mean Ronin and I were MIA when they first went to this place. Neither of us were going this time so we just had to listen. All the intel we had before today was that Aldophe and Alonia were sadistic and used magic to bind things and people to their will. Nightshade hated them from the start and I can’t say I was part of their cheering section. So when the first thing Rose reports was seeing Alonia, I think we all tensed. The self-professed Djinn never caught Rose’s position, but she also never stopped looking. That told me she was very aware someone was in the house. The last time they were at Sansbury, Snowflake hadn’t mastered the art of controlling whole buildings with his Boneyard. I don’t know why that sounds as dirty as it does when I reread it. Anyway, this time it was old hat for him and he immediately found two secret doors in dorm rooms on the top floor. What he described sounded like it used to be the master bedroom before they built a wall for the school’s use. Behind door number one was a small bedroom. Adolphe was there sitting in a chair seemingly unaware. That Snowflake could see him meant that he was corporeal. That alone gave me a bad feeling. But as there was no attempt to counter Snowflake’s scrying, he continued to the next room. Door number two was a larger bedroom with the walls painted black, furniture painted red, and a child’s skeleton on the bed. Rose slipped in and looked around without the drawbacks of Snowflakes Boneyard. The skeleton had no skin but the bones were still holding together. That gave the medic in me fits, but the Sin-Eater didn’t even blink. That’s a weird disconnect to realize in your own mind. Anyway, there was also a red bow on the skull. I couldn’t really say anything as there was an old woman in the shop at the time, but in my mind a million different torture scenes flickered by. Finally Rose noted some kind of ghostly effect on the skeleton. She opened one of the drawers in the room when a cloth doll suddenly sat up and looked at the skeleton. The skeleton of what used to be a little girl pushed its legs over the side of the bed and sat up. Then it started flailing around near the drawer. Tell me button eyes aren’t creepy now. Rose made it nearly impossible to hit her and avoided the skeleton. Snowflake then initiated what would become a battle of wills. Using his control of the Boneyard, Snowflake opened the other drawer. The skeleton ripped its arm off and hurled it in front of the drawer. Nobody really saw that coming. Then the nightstand slid against the door to block any attempt at an exit. Meanwhile the cloth doll pulled itself into a standing position, which I’m completely glad I did not see. It apparently started looking around while the skeleton continued to flail all around the room trying to attack an invisible invader. Rose was still in the room so it wasn’t too far off. They both noticed a necklace that could be a charm in the drawer.

                                                Rose left the room with her shadow mastery, but the doll came out behind her and bolted. Somehow Rose and Snowflake both knew it was headed for the Evil Magic Circle room in the basement. Snowflake immediately checked but neither ghost, as apparently Alonia was corporeal too, seemed to notice. Snowflake mentally used a hall table to bash the doll and try to stop it from going downstairs. This worked for one slam, but then the doll threw the table off. I started having Puppet Master flashbacks at that point. Thinking on his feet, Snowflake used the doll’s size and lack of fingers against it by locking all the doors around it in the hall, especially the one leading downstairs. That gave Rose and Snowflake some time to plan.

                                                With Snowflake controlling everything in the building and Rose able to move through walls, the plan became very simple. Snowflake would throw open all the doors, drawers, and anything else in Adolphe’s room then Rose could run through and get a good look at everything. Even though I had some concerns with this idea, I kept quiet...really I think I was afraid if I spoke I would give them away somehow. But when Rose bolted in the room, Adolphe had transformed into some kind of demonic battle form. He was definitely looking for a fight but still couldn’t see Rose. She got a good look at rings with the same stylistic design as the binding chest, so likely the proper keys. A red leather-bound book with a locking clasp sat inside the desk. A black cloak hung in the closet with ghosts sewn into it. In another drawer there was a long braid of ebony hair. Something other than the braiding held the thick mane together but it was the strange writing on the hardened braid that stood out. Rose took it to be Arabic in that frantic moment, which suggested it was perhaps Alonia’s.

                                                Since Adolphe seemed unable to detect Rose, Snowflake glanced back to his captive in the hallway. But to his shock, the foot tall cloth doll was carrying a sofa toward the stairwell. Using the sofa as a battering ram, the doll crashed through the door then apparently made an obscene gesture at the shattered door. Snowflake, now fully invested in this challenge, latched the lock of the broken door just to make sure the doll realized this was far from over. Snowflake described the doll as being infuriated while it ran at top speed for the basement. I don’t know how you can determine emotions on the face of a cloth doll, but I think I’m glad I wasn’t there to see it.

                                                Adolphe finally left the room to investigate the noise from the door, leaving Rose alone in the secret chamber. There was a brief moment when Snowflake and Rose both realized there was nothing stopping them from looting the entire room. Adolphe moved quickly down the stairs but apparently stopped short when Rose grabbed the braid of hair. Snowflake noted that in fact both ghosts reacted when that was touched. Both moved back toward the room, though Adolphe seemed more urgent to get there before anything happened, while Alonia was more ...eager to see if something happened. Snowflake’s description was disturbing even beyond everything else that was going on. Fortunately when he saw them both coming, he immediately told Rose to get out. She phased through the wall and meet Snowflake as Cupcake lead him away from the building. At that point I was so wound up I had to demand to know what the fuck was going on? The timing was perfect as Cindy, the 9 year old that gets candles for her grandma, had just left. All I got back was Cupcake’s very succinct description of the scene.

                                                And I quote, “It’s really scary. We’re running away!”

                                                Once he knew Rose was clear, Snowflake dropped his Boneyard and they ran for the car. I don’t know who was driving anymore, but I do remember Snowflake talking about how the whole house seemed to be angry and looking for them. There was also the doll. It was pressed...no leaned against a window on one arm and watched them (staring with its cold, dead, button eyes) drive away. Snowflake thinks that Adolphe has something bound in the doll and destroying the doll would set it free. That would likely be very bad for the DeCastros, but it’s just as likely to be bad for everyone else.

                                                Now I think we’re going to work on the triangulation. Nightshade should be here to get me soon. Ronin is at her office with Anders. The others, once they are clear of DeCastro’s reach are still on the north side of town. Hopefully this works. I need to get the shop mostly together so I can close, again. And there’s Nightshade. At least that part is always pleasant.


                                                Doc’s Log: 1650 Hours, Thursday, July 22, 2010

                                                Well that didn’t take long at all.

                                                The equipment worked the way I remembered and Nightshade thought it should. Unfortunately we figured out pretty quickly that the signal was bouncing off the atmosphere. Looking at the readings I got and hearing from the others, I figured we’d have to be within 50 feet of the actual antenna to be able to pinpoint this thing. That wasn’t what we were looking for, but you know you still have to hope.

                                                Cupcake noticed there was some really dark clouds starting to gather back towards AMC. We were trying to pretend it was just coincidence until we got back to Dark Haven Bluffs. Rose got a video message from Mongler asking, “What did you do?” We all thought this was amusing until we watched.

                                                The skies directly over Sainsbury Hall had blackened into midnight swirls of hate. Lightning arced from the Hall into the sky like stabbing blades and ball lightning spun and bounced around the building as if setting up a perimeter. Rain fell in sheets and pooled around the grounds, making the electrical fields all the more deadly. The Hall itself held an air of malice as if every window looked angrily for a target at which to last out. The entire scene pulsed with a sense of anger. Most frightening of all, just before the video ended a figure, too small to be human, climbed to the window sill on the top floor and looked directly at the camera.

                                                Talking with Mongler directly through speakerphone, we learned that the entire building somehow lurked upward about a foot. That caused such a tremor on campus that he went to investigate. His best description suggested that the house tried to jump or stand. The foundation and below were still visibly different than the rest of the wall. Even with the rain, the earth stained walls stood out. This just topped our list of things that have to be fixed...like now.


                                                Doc’s Log: 2215 Hours, Thursday, July 22, 2010

                                                This is just the day that never ends.

                                                After we got back and had dinner, we all gathered together to inspect the loot. Before anything else, I took the braid of hair because I really wanted to see what the inscription said. The hair itself had been treated in some kind of solution which hardened and generally preserved the fibers. It reminded me of petrified wood for some reason. First of all, it wasn’t written in Arabic … at least not any Arabic I’ve ever seen. It looked more like some kind of code using Arabic as the base. Having some context for what I was looking at is really the only thing that helped me. The translated phrase read, “For Binding Djinn Alonia Nazeed”

                                                We knew that rumors said Alonia wasn’t human but her claims to be a Djinn just made her sound crazier. The fact that she is a ghost means she’s clinging to memories of life. Still, given Adolphe’s penchant for binding things, I had some worrying questions. Cupcake decided to start the night of object reading with the braid so we got the sketch pad and pencils, dictionaries, and anything else we might need and she went to it. The first sketch showed how the braid was made. A weathered Arabic man chanted while treating the hair which had obviously just been cut from Alonia, who knelt in what seemed to be a catatonic state nearby. The finished braid was over a 100 years old, but that did fall within Alonia’s natural lifetime. The problems started when Cupcake tried to find out how it worked. She described her image as she saw it. Adolphe was trying to force Alonia to have sex with him while choking her with the braid. Alonia was already playing the part of Djinn, trying to kill him and seeing this as less a violation and more just another step in some kind of harsh ritual. Adolphe thought that if he could “claim” her then it would allow him to conquer her into his own service. That’s about the time Nightshade looked like she might be sick. Alonia, after having been “conquered” intended to steal Adolphe’s soul anyway. Nightshade jumped up and ran out of the room at that point. Cupcake, like the others, realized too late that this would deeply affect Nightshade. Thankfully she didn’t draw anything from the vision, as committing it to paper and creating a tangible image would have been worse, I’m sure. I went after Nightshade at that point while the others were trying to apologize. But I stopped them from following. I wasn’t rude or angry, I was in full medic mode by then. At that moment, Nightshade needed to be in a very controlled and safe environment and they were not part of that environment in this case. I’m sure that sounded terrible and Cupcake especially was beside herself for triggering such a reaction.

                                                I found Nightshade in the fetal position in her room with Karen trying to understand what just happened. I stopped at the threshold of the room, not wanting to enter the control area. Karen looked up at me and I could tell she wanted to scream at me. I’d seen that look before from friends and lovers of patients I’d had before. I called her outside so I could explain. For her part, Karen handled the situation pretty well. She seemed to knew exactly why I was reacting the way that I was and the urge to scream at me went away. At least the obvious need on her face faded. I made sure that Karen knew I’d be around tonight if or when Nightshade needed me. Otherwise, I would be around in case Karen needed anything. This might be the closest thing Karen and I can share to battle stresses. We have to be able to rely on each other to do what’s best for Nightshade. It’s times like this that I forget she’s only 20. When I remember again, I feel sorry for her just like did all the soldiers that had to learn how to survive like this at that age. But these are the choices we make to protect those that are important to us.

                                                I went back down to give the Krewe a status report and try to calm Cupcake down a little bit. This was not a moment for assigning blame. Once that was as settled as we could be, Cupcake pulled out the lock picks and popped the lock on the Big Red Book (TM). She opened the book but then she suddenly looked terrified and slammed it back quickly. We were all concerned, then she said “something” almost happened so she knew she had to close the book or else. Back to object reading again. Timing is everything after all. Cupcake figured out that opening the book without speaking a magical phrase caused a gout of fire to spray into the face of whoever opened the book and destroy the contents. The magical phrase was something else in that faux Arabic. I understood the mangled word for Master in there somewhere. Adolphe placed the spell on the book, which tells us he knows some kind of magic with which Snowflake and I aren’t remotely familiar. While I can guarantee that won’t last much longer, what the book actually contained was more disturbing yet. The names and trigger words for creatures bound to Adolphe as well as other various notes, all in that same code, were committed to the pages herein. I suspect Snowflake and I will be pouring over said pages in the days to come.

                                                Finally we came to the full length black cloak, made of fine, rich tapestry cloth. It was really nice until we saw the ghost face in the cloak. It, he just seemed to loll around unaware of much of anything anymore. Cupcake object read this as well. And while we had yet to find any redeeming qualities for the DeCastros, the history of this garment passed the final judgement for me. Adolphe wove another spell into the cloak and there were three names of entities bound into it. Only one was of a human nature. The cloak was imbued with the power to bestow the sight of Twilight to the wearer for a time when enough blood has been poured over it. Additionally, during that time of Twilight access, the wearer can will himself to be untouched by ghost, spirit, even Outsider. This made us all pause and consider what else may be bound in the well tailored cloth before us. I’m not sure if Cupcake was just desensitized by that point or was just angry, but her next bits of information really grabbed the tiger by the tail. The most recent, emotionally charged moment involving the cloak gave our diviner images of Adophe binding a woman-spider hybrid spirit into a cloth doll. Of this scene, she did sketch an image. A sexuality or feminity spirit was eaten by, more likely feed to, a spider spirit. We suspect DeCastro needed it to be able to relate on more of a human level. Snowflake stared at the image of the button-eyed doll for some time with a look that I might describe as malicious. Eventually Cupcake pulled the sketchbook back, breaking Snowflake’s concentration on the hated doll. This time she asked the cloak for it’s most emotionally charged moment of all time. I don’t think I could have inflicted such an image on myself, but Cupcake has perceived through madness time and again. It terrifies me that I can write that so casually. Anyway, she refused to draw the images she received. And in fact, she would only relay a few key points to us. There was a very bad sexual event between Alonia and something which was left as, “it was bad, don’t ask.” This awful union was merely a component of another ritual by Adolphe to summon a creature Snowflake identified with some shock as a Golmuth. This one appeared to DeCastro as “being made of Darkness with a feminine form.” Yes the d is capitalized based on the description. By all accounts, this was an Outsider which Adolphe so brazenly summoned. Given the horror stories from victims and rumors from Sainsbury Hall, nothing convinces me that the DeCastros have even slowed down in their dealings with the Outside.

                                                But they will.

                                                For most ghosts, I want to follow our mandate and help them find freedom and release them from whatever bonds chain them to this world. But in this one instance, I have become the judge and jury. The DeCastros have raped, tortured, murdered, and then bound whatever was left of countless entities through the years. There will be no pardon or release for Adolphe. There will be no unchaining of the beast that is Alonia. For them, Justice will not be merciful or swift. Their sentence will be an eternity of bondage, the same fate they have forced so many creatures into for their own pleasure. I will see this reign of slavery and torment end.

                                                This I swear in blood.


                                                Jasmine’s Diary

                                                Thursday, July 22, 2010, Afternoon

                                                I’m not meeting Jean for training again until next week, but I went to Albertus Magnus this morning to ask Mongler about our nocturnal visitor last night. He didn’t think the visitor sounded like any changelings he knew, and changelings don’t have the power to teleport like our intruder did, if only because they can only travel through doors, and our trespasser didn’t seem to do that, exactly.

                                                But Richard also wanted to talk to me about Gretchen. She’s convinced that she’s going to die eventually as a result of her research, and has told him to be prepared for that. She won’t tell him any more or let him try to help because she doesn’t want him to get tangled up in whatever is going cause her so-called “inevitable demise.” Richard is naturally quite upset and anxious about this. He loves her, and feels shut-out and helpless.

                                                So I went home and had a heart-to-heart chat with Gretchen about it. I thought maybe she was worried that Tzipporah de la Poer would eventually kill her, but there’s more to it than that. Everyone else who has ever researched the ultimate origins of the de la Poers and Fishbornes has vanished without a trace, and Gretchen assumes some dark thing from outside the balance of shadows has killed them or otherwise stolen them away to some sort of horrible elsewhere. She thinks it’s only a matter of time before the same happens to her, yet she’s not willing to abandon her research. Indeed, she thinks the only reason something hasn’t come for her is because it fears the Thing Under the House.

                                                So, I got the others involved, and we talked Gretchen into letting Richard help more. She doesn’t want him to be hurt, but shutting him out like that is selfish. And he really might be able to help protect her. After all, he has a lot of experience dealing with god-like entities from outside our world, what with the Gentry and all. She eventually relented, and I hope she really will let Richard (and us, for that matter) be a part of what she’s doing so that we can help keep her from falling to the same twisted fate that befell her predecessors.


                                                Thursday, July 22, 2010, Night

                                                What an exciting evening! I haven’t felt such an adrenaline rush since we fought the Rose Bride. I really feel like we’ve accomplished something, though I must admit a bit of trepidation over the consequences of my actions tonight.

                                                Late in the afternoon, we split into three groups to triangulate the source of the Candle Cove broadcast signal. Ultimately, we didn’t have much luck. Natalie says the signal is bouncing off the atmosphere, making it very hard to locate.

                                                However, Simon, Alexia and I formed the group going to Albertus Magnus, and we had a secondary agenda. We were going to explore Sansbury Hall more thoroughly to try and find Adolphe DeCastro’s corpse and anything else that might be helpful in sealing him and Alonia away. We never managed to find the corpse, unfortunately, but we found many other things that we hope will help us conquer the DeCastros.

                                                I drew the Shroud of Stillness around me, vanishing from sight, while Simon entered a trance just outside Sansbury Hall so that he could help me navigate the place. Alexia remained outside to watch over Simon. The place seemed deathly quiet at first, but the ghosts became aware that something was wrong fairly quickly. When I saw Alonia wandering around, looking for an intruder, I unlocked my Caul with the Stillness key so that I was little more than an invisible shadow. I remained so well hidden that neither the ghosts nor their servants ever knew exactly where I was.

                                                Alonia seemed vulnerable and harmless when I saw her, but I am pretty resistant to such charms, and easily realized that it was a trick designed put me off my guard. But since Alonia was lurking on the lower floors, I decided to avoid her at first and, at Simon’s prompting, moved upstairs, where he had detected two well-hidden secret rooms.

                                                Instead of trying to sort out how to open the secret doors, which would have given away my position anyway, I simply phased ghost-like through the walls into the rooms. The first was a small, dusty room painted in disturbing shades of black with blood red furniture. A little girl’s skeleton lay on the bed, her wispy hair still clinging to her skull, bound by a red hair bow.

                                                I quietly explored the room, and when I opened the night stand drawer, a worn rag doll sat up and looked around. It was possessed by some sort of spirit, and it immediately animated the child’s skeleton, which began flailing erratically around the room in search of the intruder. In order to avoid it, I became an incorporeal shadow, and remained that way, becoming solid again only enough to swipe what items I though might be useful to us.

                                                While the skeleton flailed around the room, I managed to steal a necklace resting in another drawer in the room. Simon helped out quite a lot by using his complete mastery over the place to open drawers and cabinets and boxes for me as I prowled about. We made a fantastic team!

                                                Eventually, the doll decided there was no one there to find, and it left the room, heading toward the ritual rooms in the basement. Simon wisely decided to keep it from going there, and used his control over the building to toss furniture on top of the doll to pin it, but it was surprisingly strong (though perhaps no stronger than a person), and was able to push the furniture off and keep going. So Simon closed and locked the doors around it, leaving it trapped.

                                                Meanwhile, I slipped into the other secret room, which was furnished like a bedroom with a wardrobe, chest, and night stands. Adolphe’s ghost was there, sitting on the bed with a look of terrible concentration on his face. He was waiting, ready to act if necessary. It would have been too risky for me to open anything myself, which would have given away my position, so Simon again helped out by simultaneously opening everything in room so that I could see what there was to see.

                                                When he did that, Adolphe transformed into a warped, twisted creature that was only vaguely human anymore. I’d heard that some ghosts could do such things, but it was still quite terrifying. With Adolphe there, I couldn’t really take anything from the room, but then the doll downstairs began tossing furniture into the doors to destroy them. The crashes drew Adolphe’s attention, and he dashed from the room to see what was wrong. I took that opportunity to snatch everything I could find in the room and then leave the building by phasing through the wall and dropping shadow-like to the ground below. I didn’t reappear until I was safely at the car with Simon and Alexia, who also fled when I told them I’d escaped with Adolphe’s possessions.

                                                I managed to escape with several items:

                                                The necklace from the doll room (which I can make into a charm).

                                                A locked book bound in red leather.

                                                A few rings inscribed with arcane sigils that match those on some of the drawers of the Obsidian Box (keys to the box?)

                                                A ritual cloak with ghosts bound into it.

                                                A bound braid of Alonia’s hair, which has been shellacked in some form of oil and has Alonia’s name painted on it in occult markings onto it.

                                                Adolphe was extremely upset when I snatched Alonia’s hair, and the entire house roared! As we made our escape, the place shuddered and shook. According to Mongler (who sent video from his phone), clouds gathered over campus shortly after we left, and a lightning storm danced around Sansbury Hall for an hour or so. The entire building seemed to be fiercely watching and looking for us. I’ve never seen a ghost so furious before! Binding Adolphe and Alonia into their anchors is not going to be easy, I’m afraid.


                                                Thursday, July 22, 2010, Late Night

                                                Alexia has had an opportunity to examine the DeCastro objects and to read a bit of their past by manifesting the Oracle. The DeCastros were terrible, horrid people. I don’t even want to write about some of the things they did. But I think the most important discovery is that Alonia isn’t a djinni at all, but was insane enough to believe she was, and somehow Adolphe used that to dominate her sexually and control her. But she always wanted to kill him in return, which we might be able to use to our advantage.


                                                Footnotes

                                                Wizards

                                                Mongler suggested that out nocturnal trespasser might be something that serves a wizard. Of course there are wizards. Why wouldn’t there be? There are vampires, werewolves, ghosts, and faeries, after all. But even saying that, wizards seem somehow more fantastic than the rest of the lot.

                                                According to Mongler, the wizards he knows of have powers much greater than those offered by the simpler occult magics that Simon and Doc have been exploring. They are more like Gandalf from the Lord of the Rings than country witches or Taoist mystics.

                                                I wonder if Maria Smith is a wizard. The intruder doesn’t seem like something she would use, but then again, we don’t really know anything about her.


                                                American Culture and Idioms

                                                Bedknobs and Broomsticks
                                                Mongler mentioned a movie called Bedknobs and Broomsticks when we were discussing the existence of wizards. No one has really told me what the movie is about, or whether it’s realistic or not, but apparently it has to do with witches and wizards.

                                                Fizban
                                                Also when we were talking about wizards, Gwen mentioned a wizard named Fizban. I’ve heard of Harry Potter and Gandalf and whoever Christopher Lee was (I’m half Kiwi, so of course I know about the Lord of the Rings…), but I’d never heard of Fizban. Apparently neither had anyone else aside from Gwen. He’s apparently a wizard from some fantasy novel she read.


                                                Protectiveness
                                                While we were discussing Mongler’s wish to help protect Gretchen, Alexia seemed to realize for the first time that Chandra has a very similar desire to protect her. She’s right, though I think the situations are also a bit different, if only because Alexia is far more capable of protecting herself than Gretchen is. Still, the sentiment is the same.

                                                Honestly, I find that sort of chivalry romantic. I love the way Doc is always standing by Natalie to watch over and protect her. It would be nice to have someone like that in my life. But it seems the Bride and I have become very good at protecting ourselves and others. I wouldn’t want Julian or Jean or even Karl putting themselves at risk for me. As romantic as the gesture might be, I wouldn’t be able to live with myself if they were hurt of killed on my behalf. Which is, of course, what Gretchen is going through. So I feel a bit hypocritical advising her to let him help. Is it so selfish to prefer your own death to that of those you love?

                                                In any case, I suppose I can justify it because unlike Jean and Julian, Mongler is more than capable of handling supernatural horrors. I can think of no one better to help keep Gretchen safe and sound.

                                                The DeCastro Objects
                                                Here are the most important things Alexia learned about the DeCastro objects:

                                                Alonia’s Hair Braid
                                                The braid was made over 100 years ago, but well within Alonia’s mortal lifetime. The hair seems to have been taken from her against her will, and Adolphe established his dominion over her by dominating her sexually while she tried to kill him. This revelation shook Natalie’s fragile psyche, and the poor girl had to leave us to cope with her own flashbacks of being raped. I felt terrible for her, but Doc assured us that it was something she would have to work through on her own.

                                                I wonder if we might be able to use the hair braid to prevent Alonia from attacking us. Maybe we can even get her to attack Adolphe.

                                                DeCastro’s Book of Binding
                                                The red-bound book was guarded by a protective fire spell that must be deactivated each time it is opened. The books contains Adolphe’s notes on the creatures he has bound and the methods for doing so.

                                                The Ritual Cloak
                                                The ritual cloak was worn by DeCastro when he conducted rituals. A ghost and two other spiritual entities are bound into the cloak. It was last used when Adolphe bound a spirit into the doll. The spirit was a spider spirit who had consumed (and fused with) a spirit of femininity or fertility or something similar. The cloak was also used when he committed a sickening ritual to summon and bind an outsider - something known as a gulmoth. He had a conversation with the gulmoth, but I we don’t now exactly what it was about.

                                                End Session XLVII

                                                I can’t stress how amused people were with the doll. Seriously. They wanted to take it home and make it a mascot.

                                                I felt that the information gathering mission went well. There seemed an appropriate amount of danger, and the PCs did a good job of using their manifestations intelligently. And I think Simon’s player got rather a charge from using Boneyard. Jasmines combination of powers to be invisible and pass through walls proved extremly effective...her player's use of Caul, Shroud and Keys is remarkably effective.

                                                Industrial Oracle is extremely handy. We have three Spooks with Oracle, but Alexia gets a lot of use with hers especially. Though Aura Perception for Gwen is always handy…though it costing Plams limits it a little.

                                                Again the DeCastro story is inspired by The Terrifying Tale of James Magnus from Ghost Stories for New World of Darkness.

                                                Natalie’s breakdown does not remove her from the game for long. She made her Resolve + Composure roll and was back in the action relatively early on in the next session. She was due for a few cracks to appear in her armor, and Alexis’s rather vivid descriptions seemed as good a place as any. She had to go back to her “clean” room at her townhouse for the night, but she was back participating the next session though fear not (or much to your consternation, depending on how you feel about Natalie I guess. )

                                                Stay tuned till next time, true believers, when the team returns to Sansbury Hall, Natalie goes on TV and they commit an act of terrorism. Next session focuses mostly on the DeCastroes, though the end relates to Candle Cove. See you then.

                                                    #58
                                                    11-22-2013, 06:54 PM
                                                    It’s that time friends, last session of 2011. Let’s see what our heroes are up to. Note Gwen is not present as her player was under the weather and had to miss.

                                                    Session XLVIII

                                                    Doc’s Log

                                                    Doc’s Log: 0230 Hours, Friday, July 23, 2010

                                                    I thought we were finished with the loot, but after I stomped off and wrote my last entry, I came back to learn of one more thing we needed to know about. It was a silver oval necklace with a smooth gem on the plate.

                                                    Cupcake did her object reading thing and learned it belonged to a dark-haired woman and possibly her daughter as well. Cupcake watched the images in her head, turned white, then green, than jerked her eyes open and started sketching. The woman, it turns out, was Denise James. The same Denise James whose ghost-ridden corpse holds Alonia’s locket in her grave. This just gets better and better. I hope I get to beat on Adolphe at least a little before we lock him in the guns. I suspect Nightshade would like a piece of Alonia too. Anyway, back to the reading. The little girl was Denise’s daughter and also the skeleton from Sainsbury Hall. This was the possible charm Rose found. I realized that it could be used for either Passion or Stillness. When Rose brought it up, Cupcake turned a little green again and immediately refused to let Rose do anything with the necklace related to Passion. Given the past of the ghosts we are dealing with, no one asked any questions.

                                                    Nightshade came back in just after that, thankfully, and was very reserved. She let us know she was going to her townhouse for the night. She already reminded me, almost apologetically, that she wouldn’t make lunch today due to recording the show for tonight. I wanted to take the right middle ground, so I assured her that I loved getting to spend my lunches with her, but just like me being gone during the day, we all have to work sometimes. That seemed to go over okay. She was definitely still not happy with the world, but she was stable. She hugged me and gave me a soft kiss before she left. I told her to call me if she needed anything. Still not going out tonight just in case.

                                                    After that, Snowflake and I took the Big Red Book (TM) down into the Library for me to translate. Cupcake made sure I knew the code word before we left. Tavia and Chandra were playing chess with Karen venting about what happened to Nightshade. Gretchen was studying in another corner of the Library. Karen looked a little sheepish when she saw me but I understand how frustrating it can be. I made sure she was actually okay, given how out there things have been. I know that’s awkward, but sometimes you need to know that someone else that understands is there when you need it.

                                                    After all the touchy-feely moments were past, I made a general announcement that no one should try to say the words I would be using as terrible things were in this book. The last thing I need is for someone to mispronounce the key as a joke and the book explode. Tavia rambled about how you have to be careful when dealing with extra dimensional things for the same reason, but sometimes getting traps like this to suck people in works really well. She stopped talking when even Snowflake could only stare in response. She takes the concept of “little sisters are weird” to a whole new level. This pseudo-code was some bastardization of Arabic and took me a little bit to get accustom to it, but after that it was just a matter of reading and writing it down in a journal that won’t involve fire to the face. I read through things before writing it down as Snowflake and I agreed that some things didn’t need to be readily available. Usually if I had to have him help me understand what a translation meant, I didn’t write it down. The short version of the notes were scary enough as it is. There was some mostly useless blackmail information on all the neighbors at the time. That was at least slightly amusing. Then we found various people Adolphe had programmed to gather his stuff in case he ever vanished. That was a longer list than I was comfortable with. However Denise was on that list. As was Deacon James, her husband (we think), who was to come and collect several of Adolphe’s more important personal affects. This could easily be where the pistols are. My blood ran cold when I realized the address was Deanna’s house. Clarissa Walthstone’s friend Deanna James might actually be the key to removing Adolphe and Alonia. That was truly terrifying.

                                                    We learned, though it wasn’t as surprising as I thought at first, that Adolphe occasionally traded books with one Erasmus Fishborn. Snowflake nodded and pointed to the old family tree that was now hanging on the Library wall. Erasmus was several greats back, but he was there...listed as having lost himself to his work in ‘49. Erasmus was also on the list of people to collect Adolphe’s things, though not on the programmed list. He was charged with damage control, looking after the books, and keeping people out of the house that we know as Sainsbury Hall. Erasmus had a wife, Petunia, that was noted for gnawing on Alonia at social functions. Adolphe listed in his notes that Alonia should take it so personally as Petunia does this to alot of people. Snowflake filled me in that Petunia had problems with enjoying the taste of flesh a little too much. She developed the habit of gnawing on people to help keep the problems down. But after the second child when missing, they put her in a sanitarium. Well that’s not what he said, but that’s what I think he meant. Either way, she was listed on the family tree as having died of Hoof and Mouth disease in ‘36. I didn’t really believe any part of that, but by this point, I knew better than to ask. Whatever part of Erasmus’ work he was lost to led to the Rat Section of the House being closed off. Tavia noted that he was one of the Rat Talkers in the family, like Alistair. Enough said there. I also found strange recipes for what looked like perfectly normal shoe shine/polish and laundry instructions written in the side margins of the book of evil. There is no fucking way this is legit. I don’t know what kind of code this is, but I will break it. Snowflake might have a healthy obsession with taking control of all Adolphe’s possessions, but I will know his secrets. And when I do, I will lay them bare and use them against him.

                                                    As I could tell Gretchen was trying to spy on us, like a curious little kid, I took a break and pulled her into the conversation. We talked about her research and I tried to get her to accept our help officially. The best I could do is get her to agree to let us know when she thought she was on to something. The whole bit about Mongler being worried didn’t sit completely well with her, but it did lead into an interesting discovery about Snowflake. He has apparently learned a ceremony which allows a way to cheat Death. He called it the Devil’s Bargain, which was appropriately sinister for something that can extend your life. The short version is that someone has to willingly give up years of their life so that another can be granted that same amount of time. If the recipient would normally die during that time, they live on until the time runs out. If they are killed during that time, they live on as well. I’d think it was much worse if it didn’t involve the willing transfer of years. I’m going to make sure neither Rose or Nightshade hear about this, but for very different reasons. Gretchen thought this was a great way to make money. Using it as an insurance policy, parents would gladly through their life away to guarantee their children would live on. I gave her a dirty look that I think Rose would have approved of...I mean damn. This is what could make it much worse.

                                                    After that I realized what time it was and decided to call it a night. I made sure my phone was on charge and the ringer up just in case Nightshade needed me. Now that I have this written out, I think I’m going to sleep.


                                                    Doc’s Log: 1015 Hours, Friday, July 23, 2010

                                                    I woke up in a brief panic that Nightshade had called and I didn’t hear. But I checked the phone and found nothing was waiting and no missed calls. Meditation took about an hour this morning, but I got up early, so no big deal.

                                                    Snowflake was up for breakfast this morning, which was odd. He was all dressed up and awake which I didn’t understand at first. But before I left, he let me know he was going to go and meditate in his grave. I have no idea why but it somehow makes him feel more confident afterwards. Maybe I’ll ask him to explain to me someday.

                                                    After that I went to work. And it’s been a slow morning. I had a group of guys come in to get supplies before they left for a camping trip this weekend, but after that it’s been pretty quiet. We’ll see how long that lasts.


                                                    Doc’s Log: 1425 Hours, Friday, July 23, 2010

                                                    About the time Nightshade would normally stop in for lunch, I started getting a stream of random texts and pics on my phone. I’m pretty sure she was nervous and releasing tension. It was really cute. She was flustered about the wig she wanted to wear and this or that about her clothes. I told her she was going to be gorgeous in any outfit she put on. While I think she appreciated the sentiment, it wasn’t terribly useful in helping her sort out her dilemma. There was a lull in texting and then I got one from Karen telling me to tell Nightshade not to wear a damn wig. This was really funny to me, but I wisely kept that to myself. I got another text from Karen a few minutes later telling me that Gretchen was running around in a school girl uniform. Without the “benefit” of hearing Gretchen, that was briefly a nice thought.

                                                    Then we settled down as Rose, Snowflake and Cupcake were going back out to Sainsbury Hall to check for bodies underground. They were in a little too much of a hurry to get away before to really do much with it. Ear curls in place, Rose called Mongler to let him know they were coming. He didn’t think this was a great idea since the house was still visibly angry, but Rose assured him they weren’t going to be in sight of the house this time. I really hate every recon mission we go on these days. As soon as they got settled a building or so away, Snowflake let us know he found the bodies. Yeah that’s not singular. There were eight bodies buried below the basement. Nothing gave us any clue as to which one, if any, were Adolphe or Alonia. To make matters worse, Snowflake found a cavern under the house. The underground river that feeds AMC connected to a cave leading to the cavern. In the cave there as a 16’ long black blob with two huge eyes and no face. It was making noises that Snowflake could really only compare to a crying baby. He had no idea what it was, other than the strong possibility of an Outsider. That was enough for the recon. Not much any of us can do with the house looking to eat us and a monster underground.

                                                    That lead to our discussion of trying to use the braid to resolve Alonia’s connection to Adolphe’s body. That way at least we could force her away from Adolphe while we either deal with him or try to take care of her and Denise. Either way, it was something we weren’t ready to do right then. The headed back to the Bluffs and I went back to drawing.

                                                    Hopefully tonight will work out better.


                                                    Doc’s Log: 2245 Hours, Friday, July 23, 2010

                                                    We did it!

                                                    Rose, Snowflake, Nightshade, and Ronin headed out with the materials, while I walked into town with my Phantasmal Shroud up. For once, I could be sure no one that saw me would know who I was. Wearing gloves so there would be no prints, I placed the call from a pay phone. Then I caught a cab back to a random house out past Malard’s. Through the curl, I could hear as Snowflake killed the security, then Rose and Nightshade snuck in. I’m pretty sure Ronin was mumbling to herself about how she could find a way to make security against this kind of thing. She just does not give up. Anyway, they all managed to do their thing without a hitch and Ronin got them the hell out of there before anyone was the wiser. We all met back at the House where Cupcake had prepared Post-Bomb threat Brownies.

                                                    Now we’re just winding down from the adrenaline and nerves. All in all, I think this was a good day.


                                                    Jasmine’s Diary

                                                    Friday, July 23, 2010, Morning

                                                    I had an especially good meditation this morning. I reached such a transcendent sense of calm and tranquility that I almost felt as though all was right with the world. I haven’t been so at peace since Richard died.

                                                    Last night, Doc and Simon did some research using Adolphe’s journal. They discovered a lot of blackmail material on the DeCastro’s neighbors, and that the DeCastros had borrowed books from Erasmus Fishborne, one of Simon’s forebears (but that’s hardly surprising, now is it?). They also learned more about Denise James, the woman who was buried with Alonia’s locket and whose necklace we found in the child’s room in Sansbury Hall. Denise and Deacon James were friends of the DeCastros, and the DeCastro’s must have “programmed” them to remove certain items from Sansbury hall when the DeCastros died. We think they must have Adolphe’s pistols, so we need to find a way to get them. The problem is that Denise and Deacon are the great-great grandparents of Deanna James, who is Clarissa’s friend (Clarissa the psychokinetic). Actually this might be a good thing, since we sort of know her, but also bad because we’d rather she not become tangled up in all of this.


                                                    Friday, July 23, 2010, Early Afternoon

                                                    Natalie came home today, and I was glad to see she was doing much better than when she left last night. She seems to have managed to put down the traumatic memories triggered by Alexia’s descriptions of the DeCastro’s horrid activities. I think this is a good sign. She seem to be recovering, and regaining more psychological stability. Doc’s been a good influence on her, I’m sure.

                                                    When she arrived home, she ran across Simon and I chatting, and announced that she was going to go play piano. I think we both got the impression that an audience would be welcome, so we went and listened. She mostly played swing songs like the Andrews Sisters, which suited me just fine. And she played them well.

                                                    After that, she had to go get ready for her show. She hadn’t talked much to me about it aside from a few words over dinner or breakfast (I think it’s something that came up while I was in Taiwan), but she’s filming host segments for a sort of vintage horror movie show that will be aired later tonight. I certainly wished her luck. It sounds like just her thing, and I’m sure she’ll perform wonderfully. Unlike some of us, she likes being on camera.

                                                    Doc is at work, Gwen’s off on a case again, and Natalie is filming now. So it’s up to Simon, Alexia and I to investigate Sansbury Hall more this afternoon.


                                                    Friday, July 23, 2010, Late Afternoon

                                                    Natalie’s filming was a success. She seems pleased with it, anyway. I suppose we’ll all watch her show tonight. I don’t expect to enjoy the movie, but I’d like to support Natalie, at least.

                                                    Simon, Alexia and I returned to Sansbury Hall this afternoon. I called Mongler to let him know, and he was a bit anxious about it since the building is still agitated. We didn’t go within sight of the building, and remained hidden behind a neighboring dormitory. I was prepared to venture into the place under the cloak of my Shroud again, but it wasn’t necessary. Simon unlocked his Boneyard with the Grave-Dirt Key and scanned the grounds for buried bodies.

                                                    Unfortunately, he found eight of them. And we have no idea which belongs to Adolphe. That’s not all, though. There’s an underground river running beneath Albertus Magnus, and it run close to Sansbury Hall’s foundations. Near it is a sealed chamber that contains some sort of outsider beast that Simon described as a black, shadowy blob with two huge eyes and moth that mewed like a whimpering baby. I’m not one to curse, but I really want to right now. This haunting is becoming more and more tangled all the time, and I intensely dislike the DeCastros meddling with outsiders, summoning them into this world where they certainly don’t belong. If I weren’t so centered from my morning mediatations, I think I would be really, really angry about all of this. Angry enough to want to destroy Adolphe DeCastro utterly. But as it is, I’m simply resolved to end the stain of his abominable practices once and for all.

                                                    We left the area of Sansbury Hall then. Simon’s going to do more research in Adolphe’s book. I called Mongler to let him know what we had found, and we discussed the krewe’s plans to seal the DeCastro’s into their anchors. He didn’t like that idea, and warned that things that are sealed away often find a way to escape eventually, though it may take centuries. I assured him that we really didn’t have any other options in this case, because we aren’t going to be able to resolve Adolphe and Alonia peacefully.

                                                    After returning home, Simon called Deanna to see if she’d be willing to help us out. I didn’t think it was a good idea to get her involved, but Simon managed to engage her interest by suggesting that she had an opportunity to become involved in something paranormal, not unlike her friend Clarissa. I was a bit cross with Simon after his conversation, and I made him promise not to lead the poor girl on with false promises.

                                                    In any event, Deanna was a bit wary. She doesn’t really know us. So she and Simon are going to meet at a café tomorrow to talk about it more.

                                                    I stayed on campus for awhile and swam. It was so refreshing to get back into the pool and to lose myself to in the water again. After my meditation today and all the frustrations of dealing with the DeCastros, I feel a strong desire to lose myself deep in the ocean like I did when the Bride was helping me grasp the Primeval Key.


                                                    Friday, July 23, 2010, Night

                                                    We executed the krewe’s ill-advised plan to stage a terrorist bombing on the radio-station today. I’m still against it, but I didn’t have much choice but to help them, since the rest of them had made up their minds, and they needed someone to sneak in and plant the fake bomb. I’m really the only one who could pull that off, so I did it, but I’m not proud of it.

                                                    The rest of the plan went off well. Doc placed an anonymous bomb-threat call and once the radio station was cleared out, Natalie slipped inside to program the jamming signal that we hope with shut down the Candle Cove broadcast for a time.

                                                    I’m glad that’s over with.


                                                    Footnotes

                                                    The Necklace

                                                    Last night, after Alexia finished reading the DeCastro objects through her oracle and Doc and Simon disappeared to do some research, Alexia and I set about turning the necklace I liberated from Sansbury Hall into a charm. According to Alexia, it once belonged to a friend of Alonia’s named Denise James - the same Denise James who we believe may have helped store some of the DeCastro’s things, and who is currently buried with the Alonia’s locket. The little girl’s skeleton probably belonged to Denise’s daughter.

                                                    With Alexia’s help, I threaded some black ribbon through the necklace’s chain and turned it into a charm of Stillness. I wanted to make something for one of the other krewe members, but Stillness was the best fit for the necklace, so I’ll keep it for now. If any of the others need it, I’ll be happy to let them use it.

                                                    Spooky

                                                    Since I got back from Taiwan, Natalie has seemed much more pleased to see me. I get the impression that she really missed me when I was gone. I confess I missed her too. For all of her rough edges, she is a part of my family—the krewe. Maybe the time apart did us both some good.

                                                    She’s also been calling me “Spooky” lately. The nickname’s obviously calling attention to my ghostly appearance and morose personality—more of the “goth” thing Natalie has attributed to me—so I feel like I should be annoyed by the nickname, but I’m not. Not at all. In fact, I like it. It’s an affectionate nickname, and makes me feel loved. And maybe I’ve come to accept that I really am a bit spooky and “gothic.”


                                                    Natalie’s Tweets

                                                    -I’m awake. Sleeping alone is terrible, but last night I really had to. Even an innocent sleep with another person is usually better than alone.

                                                    End Session XLVIII

                                                    OK well that was fun.

                                                    Not much to report on not covered. There was a lot of OOC chatter so less got done, but it was still a good session. Jasmine's player got a really good Meditation roll...I forget the number of successes but it was amazing. Thus Jasmine was rather laid back much of the session. Natalie was playing piano as much of her time as a young girl she played a lot of Andrews Sisters and some Christian Rock, Amy Grant type stuff...if you can imagine. Now she does not feel...comfortable with some of that but the Andrews Sisters music is still a comfort to her, as she noted in session.

                                                    She also, wanting to look different, wound up in blue jeans, white t-shirt, denim jacket and red bandanna to sneak into the station. It was about as different as I could think of.

                                                    Also, once again got to say skin crawlingly creepy/dirty things IC as Gretchen. That is always fun. Also from Tavia "Remember when we were little and thought Great Aunt Petunia was going to rise from the dead to eat us? Remember when she did? That was scary, we should not have played near her sarcophagus!"

                                                    Stay tuned for next time, when the Krewe gets a much better idea of the story behind the intruder, Natalie sends out a large number of hilariously sleep dep'd tweets, and the session ends in a cliffhanger. Be there or, uh, be somewhere else I suppose.
