                                                        #59
                                                        11-22-2013, 07:00 PM
                                                        The last session was an exciting session, to be sure. Let’s see what 3 of our Krewe members had to say.

                                                        Session XLIX

                                                        Doc’s Log

                                                        Doc’s Log: 0435 Hours, Saturday, July 24, 2010

                                                        The Post Bomb-threat Brownies were had while we watched Nightshade’s show. The movie was some terribly low-budget director’s indulgence. I think it was called Werewolf, but the main female lead was trying to use a Swedish (I hope that’s what she was trying for) accent so it was Wherewoffa, Verevolf, warenwoof, and others that my mind refuses to remember. Nightshade said before it started that she would not be upset if we thought the movie was terrible. And it was. Rose was really scandalized by Nightshade’s dress or lack of it. I had to try very hard not to laugh. We all forgot she has never seen one of these Friday Night Monster Flicks. Thus we explained that there has to be a really scantily clad hostess to keep the people interested, because the movie sure won’t do it. She agreed but I don’t think she could really get past how much cleavage Nightshade was showing. It was quite a bit more than usual but no one else seemed to have an issue.

                                                        After we suffered through the movie and enjoyed Nightshade’s commentary, both on-screen and off, we got back to work. We had to plan how to deal with Deanna and her link to the DeCastros. The major hold up came in that most people didn’t think the girl was mature enough to be told anything real for fear that she would do something stupid and get herself killed or worse. Eventually we settled on just getting her out of the house, learning if anyone else was home, and then moving to sack the house. Nightshade sent Deanna a text to meet for coffee later today. That would get her out and talking. Then Rose and Snowflake could check the house if it was clear. I checked the maps and made sure on distances, but the James house was close enough to the Firehouse Church that Rose could teleport herself. So if we found the guns and anything else, we could move them quickly to a place we could secure and defend if necessary. Then we’ll need to move on Sainsbury to break the link to Adolphe holds to the dorm. All while holding Alonia off. No sweat.

                                                        With that plan in place, Snowflake and Jasmine went out to hit the Haunts for Plasm around 0100 Hours. Karen and I went to the Piano room to listen to Nightshade play. We’d been talking about old songs, listening to Nightshade, all in all a few hours when Rose and Snowflake got back. Rose looked confused and exhausted. She told us that they found our intruder, or rather he found them at AMC. The music of course stopped but Rose had no intention of telling the story several times and she was up way past her bedtime. She assured me that she and Snowflake were okay and this would keep til morning. With that she left us to go to bed. Nightshade decided that she would stay up til breakfast. I don’t see this going well, but for now I’m going to bed myself.


                                                        Doc’s Log: 1015 Hours, Saturday, July 24, 2010

                                                        I didn’t have to go and open the shop so I was a little lazy this morning. I finally rolled out of bed around 0815 and started Meditation. Before I knew it, I was back downstairs for breakfast by 0900. Some days meditation is just like that.
                                                        Nightshade was at breakfast and about as keyed up as I was afraid she’d be. But Rose finally decided to fill us in on last night’s events. Apparently she spotted something jumping to the top of a dorm at AMC before they could leave. She tried to follow it, but it seemed to know where she was at all times. Rose is not use to things knowing where she is when she doesn’t want them too so I think she saw this as a challenge. She left Snowflake, which was probably not the best idea, and turned into a flock of birds to chase our intruder down. She thought she had him cornered before he turned into mist and flowed through a window sill into another building. Rose’s determination is sometimes its own liability. This was the case last night it seems as she crashed through the window still as a flock of birds and swooped to get close to stranger. He promptly vanished. Snowflake, after being abandoned on a spectrally hostile campus, sat down and unlocked a Boneyard so he might have some idea what was happening. He followed the chase and even heard our intruder utter a phrase in Mandarin before disappearing. Rose has translated and it means, “How the devil did she do that?” The great mystery from last night was revealed when Rose explained she now knew what and possibly who this Jiang Shi was. Since none of us had a clue what she was talking about, Rose gave us the Chinese vampire 101 lesson. Chinese vampires are really nothing like western vampires at all. I should probably be shocked but after learning about Chandra this was almost expected. They aren’t Embraced like the vampires I know or even infected like a Penagallen. These creatures are the results of certain curses, specific rituals, and strangely also occur randomly from bad burials. They also aren’t like western vampires due to possessing anchors like ghosts, which along with their graves, they can teleport between. Ronin must have thought it the same time I did as she started staring at Rose and becoming more and more annoyed. Finally, after Ronin’s normal morning grumbling turned aggressive, she announced that Rose was an anchor. With that out in the open, we decided to pump Rose for all the info she could tell us. Jiang Shi drink blood and have anchors like ghosts. They are paralyzed in sunlight, not destroyed by it. They have no good way to make others and they can’t be resolved like a ghost can. Lovely.

                                                        It was about this time that I took Nightshade up to her room and put her to bed. She was wobbling and not really getting it anymore. I came back down to hear our first Mandarin lession will be to make sign outside to try and convince this thing to talk to us. Oh yeah, important note, we now think this Jiang Shi is either Rose’s uncle Guang or working for him. The plan is now to make signs saying, “Attention Jiang Shi. Would like to speak.” It was followed by Rose’s real name but that would defeat the point of having nicknames. I have no idea what I’m writing now, I’m just following what Rose has drawn in front of me. Now I need to get to it.


                                                        Doc’s Log: 1400 Hours, Saturday, July 24, 2010

                                                        After we made up the signs for Rose, a trip to the Library was in order. Snowflake, always the one to find horrible rituals, found one that would change a Jiang Shi into a “normal” vampire, they just have to eat the soul of the western vampire, losing all Jiang Shi powers and becoming whatever type of vampire the western one was originally. They are very instinctual but not necessarily mindless, which works in our favor. Apparently they can turn into a large bird, likely why it was confused when Rose did the whole murder thing last night. A bit of good news is they can’t be bound by blood of western vampires. The less good news is that they are hard to kill. They must be completely destroyed by fire to kill them outright. They are also tough, strong, and fast like most vampire legends. Hopefully if this is Guang, it won’t come to that.


                                                        Doc’s Log: 1630 Hours, Saturday, July 24, 2010

                                                        Since everyone else is taking their Mary Sweet Time!, I might as well give an update so far.

                                                        Nightshade got up about 1430 Hours so we all prepared to finally meet the infamous Adolphe DeCastro. But there was no guarantee we’d find anything, so things could have gone very boring. Honestly I don’t think that ever happens to people that have already died once.

                                                        I got my knife and Cupcake drove over to the Firehouse church. Ronin took Rose and Snowflake to the ancestral James home while Nightshade chatted Deanna up at a coffee shop. Cupcake and I were really just here as backup since I checked the map and Rose would be able to teleport here from the house. We all had the ear curls in to keep up with the situations in all locations. After a few minutes Ronin scared the shit outta me when she started looking for signs of ghostly power. She didn’t find anything except Rose’s powers, but she didn’t say that at first. I informed her she was a shithead. Then things went downhill. Snowflake was outside keeping watch through Boneyard. He found a painting sealed into the wall, but there was no light so he couldn’t see what it was. Ronin found Deanna’s weed stash. Figures. Somehow, Rose managed to slip into the wall space and look at he painting. It was very similar to one of Tavia’s Outsider paintings. She described it as an open night sky, with dark figure, back to the viewer, on a hill in the distance. She said she felt and urge to step into the painting and go speak to the dark stranger. We didn’t have what we needed to deal with that, but at least with it sealed in the wall, it’s as safe as it could be. Next they moved into the attic. You ever notice how nothing good is ever in an attic? They found a large trunk that required two keys. Simon handled that rather easily with his control of the area. There was another chest inside. Inside the chest was our missing pistol case, an arm-length stick with strange symbols carved end to end and a loop on the end, plus a pair of brass rings with a symbol on each of them. This was the jackpot.

                                                        Rose says she’s in the church now so I should probably loo...FUCK!

                                                        The skies overhead just turned as black as midnight and twice as churning. I think we might have gotten some attention X~’ \l

                                                        Damn it, Adolphe is here and everyone is screaming for me to leave him be. He’s going for his guns and Rose is still in there with him. The wind out here is picking up like a hurricane is coming. I’m putting up Stigmata Shroud because this will not end without some kind of a fight. Cupcake is worried that someone will get possessed and take the guns out or just take the whole lockbox. That’s a good point. I’ve told Ronin to get more chains and locks so we can effectively bolt the thing down to a shelf or the wall.

                                                        This is not the way I expected this to play out. And they still won’t let me at least distract him. Nightshade is better at the whole ghost command but I know I can do this. Hell I’m only still writing this because if I don’t all I can do it stare at an open door.

                                                        Nightshade just got here and has a new plan. She’s going to ward him inside and lock him down for a few days, then we can go deal with his body. Rose is actually outside with us, finally so I don’t have to worry about that right now. I like this plan, but we still need to lock those pistols down more securely. Ronin has the chains and everything so she’s going to go in and move for the guns while Rose and I keep this asshole busy.

                                                        Nightshade, I’m leaving this with you so I don’t make it out, you already know I love you. Be safe.


                                                        Jasmine’s Diary

                                                        Friday, July 23, 2010, Late Night

                                                        After watching Natalie’s show earlier tonight, we discussed what to do about the DeCastros. Simon and Natalie thought that Deanna’s parents were away most weekends, and so Natalie and Simon offered to go and chat with Deanna tomorrow at a café, luring her out of the house so that Gwen and I could sneak in and look for the DeCastro pistols. Once we had them, we feared Adolphe’s ghost would come to see what was happening to his anchor, so to keep him away from Deanna’s place, I would then get them to the firehouse church (which we now own and which is very isolated) as quickly as possible. Doc and Alexia would be waiting at the church to help out against Adolphe once I arrived, and we would lock the pistols away in a lockbox there. This way, Adolphe won’t be able to harm Deanna’s family once we sever his connection to Sansbury Hall. Then it will be a matter of sealing him into his pistols.

                                                        Doc and Simon are also researching whether it’s possible to sever Alonia’s connection to Adolphe by destroying her braid, since it’s the ritual object that tied her to him in the first place. If we can do that, then we won’t have to deal with her in Sansbury Hall. We can resolve her at her grave, without Adolphe there to interfere.

                                                        In order to prepare for tomorrow, Simon and I decided to go prowling around some of our haunts to draw off some plasm, as we were both a bit low, and knew we might be spending a lot tomorrow. We briefly stopped into Murder Ballads and then went to AMC to visit Nilan and Rose Halls. It was while we were on our way between them that I happened to notice a figure in the shadow of a nearby building.

                                                        When it realized I was looking at it, it fled, and made an amazing leap onto the top of a dormitory. I saw more of it then, and recognized the gray skin that Alexia attributed to our intruder of a few nights ago. I quickly gave chase with a short warning to Simon, who hadn’t seen the thing and was a bit confused.

                                                        It became quickly obvious that I wouldn’t be able to catch up to it on foot, so I transformed into a flock of crows and flew after it. It knew exactly where I was, and ducked into a building by phasing through a wall, not unlike what I can do. Determined to follow it, I flew in after it, smashing through a window. I was going to reform into my normal body and confront it, but it uttered a brief Chinese curse and disappeared. Before it vanished, I noticed that much of its face was hidden by a cloth wrapped around it, as though it was trying to conceal its features, and it was dressed in modern Taiwanese factory-worker’s clothing. That struck me as very odd until I put the pieces together and realized what it all meant.

                                                        I returned to Simon to find him sprawled out on the campus lawn in a Boneyard trance. I shook him out of it, and asked if he’d seen anything I hadn’t. He was only able to confirm that it seemed to have a supernatural sense of my location, and that when I turned into crows, it muttered, “How the devil did she do that!?” (in Mandarin, which Simon was able to repeat back with some difficulty).

                                                        I knew then what the thing was, and had a suspicion about why it was here. I briefly filled Simon in, but decided to wait until tomorrow morning to give all the details, as I don’t think we’re in any danger from it, and I wanted to tell everyone at once. Unfortunately, this meant convincing Natalie to be up early, and she decided to just stay awake through the night. She doesn’t deal well with lack of sleep, which surprises me.

                                                        Here is my theory:

                                                        The intruder is a Jiang Shi, a type of Chinese vampire-ghost. It knew where I was because I’m its anchor, which works much like a true ghost’s anchor. And I believe that the Jiang Shi either is my uncle Guang who disappeared recently, or is working for him. It’s far more likely that it really is my uncle, though, since it seems to want to hide from me., like it doesn’t want to be recognized. Because I’m it’s only anchor here, it can only appear near me, but it probably doesn’t dare appear in the house anymore since the house tried to kill it. I presume it has a grave of some sort in Taiwan, so it can return there at will, but it seems to be curious about New Haven.

                                                        I need to find out what it’s after and why I’m its anchor. If it is my uncle Guang, then he’s become attached to me for a reason, and it might have to do with his death. I’d like to help him if possible, but I need to talk to him first. I think I’ll make some signs that say, in Mandarin, “Jiang Shi. Uncle Guang. Please talk to me. Xiao Ming.” I’ll put them up outside the house and maybe one in my office at AMC. Hopefuly that will encourage him to approach me instead of fleeing.


                                                        Saturday, July 24, 2010, Noon

                                                        I had a good meditation this morning, though not as good as yesterday. Simon and I discussed our encounter with the Jiang Shi with the others over breakfast, and I shared my theories. I don’t think the Jiang Shi means us any harm, but it wasn’t easy to convince Gwen of that. After breakfast, Doc, Simon, Gwen and I went down to the library to research Jiang Shi more, and learned a bit a more than what I already knew from growing up in Taiwan.


                                                        Saturday, July 24, 2010, Afternoon
                                                        I’m writing this in Gwen’s SUV. We’re driving at breakneck speeds to AMC, where Adolphe has unleashed the amorphous shadow creature that he had stored beneath Sansbury Hall, and that we discovered in our follow-up investigations there.

                                                        I don’t have long to write, and Gwen’s driving makes it very hard to keep my pen steady, so I’ll have to be brief. Our plan for acquiring the pistols worked very well. Simon, Gwen and I went to the James’ house and while Simon waited outside, Gwen and I broke in and searched for the pistols, which took far longer than we would have liked.

                                                        In the process, Simon sensed a painting sealed away in the narrow space between two walls. I phased in to have a look at it, which was not easy, and discovered that it was much like the Outsider paintings Tavia has painted. It pictured a nighttime landscape with a lonely figure on the crest of a hill. I felt compelled to enter the painting and join that figure there, but I shook off the comuplsion and phased out of the wall. For now, the painting’s probably as safe as it’s going to be, and we have other things to worry about.

                                                        We finally found the pistols, and I took them, box and all, and phased directly out of the upper-story attic, then, as I fell toward the ground, I used my Cold Wind Caul to become one with the winds and disappear. A minute later, I rematerialized outside the firehouse church. I was invisible the whole time, of course, so I had to tell Doc and Alexia that I’d arrived so that they would be prepared. Poor Alexia seemed terribly nervous, but I knew she would stand firm if things got bad, and Doc looked like he was ready start a fight.

                                                        I ran to the basement and locked the pistols away in a lockbox. Meanwhile, the skies above the firehouse church filled with roiling black clouds and lightning, and then a vapour spiraled down from the clouds and coalesced into a humanoid shape. It was Adolphe, and he ignored all of us, even Doc’s bravado, and strode directly toward the pistols. Alexia got out of his way, and so did Doc, wisely.

                                                        Gwen arrived about then with a safe and chains to lock the pistols away and make them more secure. Natalie drove up then as well, worried sick about Doc (and for good reason, as I thought Doc might decide to attack Adolphe then and there, the way he was stomping about). She cut herself to create a stigmatic ward that would trap Adolphe at the church for a few days, which we hope will prevent him from possessing anyone or causing any more trouble than he already has.

                                                        But as Gwen began locking away the pistols, Adolphe told us in voice that boomed like thunder that to punish us for the theft of his pistols, he had unleashed the Abyssal beast he kept hidden beneath Sansbury Hall. We have to get there as quickly as possible and stop it before it does any more harm than it already has. I hope we’re not too late. I hope I can reach Mongler!

                                                        Jiang Shi

                                                        Here’s what I remember about Jiang Shi from growing up in Taiwan (and being fascinated by the occult even as child):

                                                        Jiang Shi are a form of Chinese vampire, but they have a lot in common with ghosts too, in that they are bound to their grave and possibly other anchors.

                                                        Like ghosts, they can manifest near their anchors, but once manifested, they are physical beings.

                                                        Jiang Shi aren’t “infected” like western vampires. Instead, they are created by esoteric rituals or, more commonly, when a person is not properly buried.

                                                        They are sometimes created as servants to those practiced in the occult.

                                                        They are sometimes called “hopping vampires” because they are capable of impressive leaps. Some legends and popular fictions say they bunny hop everywhere, but that’s not true.

                                                        And here’s the additional information we learned with our research:

                                                        Jiang Shi can turn into birds (instead of bats, like western vampires).

                                                        They can consume the soul of a western vampire (which is something all western vampires can do as well, though fortunately they consider it a terrible act), and by doing so, they become a western vampire, permanently taking on the qualities of whatever type of vampire they consumed.

                                                        Unlike western vampires, they cannot be bound to the power of another’s blood (becoming the slave of that vampire), but cannot bind others either.

                                                        They are difficult to destroy unless they are killed with fire, and tend to disappear back to their graves if threatened with destruction.

                                                            Alright, ready for an action-packed session? It is action packed.

                                                            Session L

                                                            Doc’s Log

                                                            Doc’s Log: 2025 Hours, Saturday, July 24, 2010

                                                            Welcome to Clusterfucksville.

                                                            That’s not exactly accurate considering we all lived and were more successful than we expected. Actually, locking an asshole Yellow Bones in an abandoned firehouse and destroying an Outsider is pretty good for a day’s work. I’m just tired and grumpy because of the price we paid and I’m well past coming off the adrenaline high.

                                                            After I finished freaking out before, Ronin locked the guns down before we all jumped back in a car. Ronin drove Rose and Cupcake like a bat outta hell. Snowflake drove Nightshade’s car as she was already experiencing mild delirium from Hemodynamic shock. Even with my training, seeing Nightshade sitting in that big of a pool of her own blood shook me up. The best I could manage was to be angry things got that far as I destroyed my shirt to bandage her as best I could. She keeps her tools clean, but she doesn’t make shallow incisions when she’s in a hurry. Ronin called Chuck on the way and called for a full scale lock down of AMC while Rose tried to call Mongler. I remember thinking it was more weird that we couldn’t find the Faerie quickly than it was that Ronin could just call in a lock down of a public institution. Yeah I don’t know either.

                                                            Mongler eventually got back to Rose so she gave him the Incoming report. I was upset that Nightshade had wounded herself that much, mainly because I couldn’t help her right then. Snowflake got us there as quickly as he could, but it was a long drive for me. I could have blown my energy healing Nightshade, but then I’d be next to useless in the coming fight. She kept telling me to calm down but then saying things about how pretty the blood was. Snowflake was rightly concerned since he was driving someone else’s car and that someone else was covered in her own blood in the back seat. The cops would have truly made this a bad situation so I was glad we managed to get through before the police barricades went up. Ronin may have had something to do with that too for all I know. When I could see the entrance to campus, I put up my Primeval Shroud to be prepared for anything. When the car stopped, I opened the door and The Dog of War leapt out. That was cool and all, but then I had to go back and get my ear curl. Less awesome.

                                                            There was no noise on campus. The birds were gone, the wind was still, and even the bugs were quiet. That is never a good sign. The movies actually get that one right. Mongler arrived shortly in an camper squealing tires as he skidded to a stop followed by two patrol cars which slid into a Y behind him. This was really loud given how still campus was, so we all knew something was happening. I didn’t go rushing around as I didn’t need cops to shoot me for being a monster. Mongler did something, then we heard a man scream and one of the patrol cars peeled out. Snowflake stayed with Nightshade at the car. He dropped into a Boneyard trance and immediately gave us bad news while I went to find Rose, Ronin, and Cupcake. This Outsider that DeCastro released didn’t go very far. Actually it just possessed the whole campus. So it was in effect doing something similar to Snowflake’s Boneyard. I focused my anger through my Shroud and felt myself become stronger and faster. I was no longer simply trying to protect...I NEEDED to fight. Primeval seems to do that as when I found Rose, she was similarly in her Feline Caul stalker form with Ronin and Cupcake following behind. With one blood-covered goth girl, my comatose body, and one kid laying unconscious in a car, two animal monsters stalking around, one gun wielding ex-cop, one chef with six knife-waving arms, a Faerie dinosaur-man, and a creature from Beyond the universe, Albertus Magnus was officially the most horrifying location in the whole state of Connecticut, if not a much larger area. Sadly with all that we brought to bear, there was no target. Ronin grabbed her phone and called Dark Haven Bluffs. She got Tavia on the phone and told her to go look up anything in the Library that might help us. She gave all the information we knew and Tavia immediately went to work. She seemed to have a better idea what we were dealing with just based on that description than the rest of us. I forget that Tavia is so very dangerous in some ways.

                                                            While we researched by proxy, Rose decided that we animal people should go and investigate Sainsbury Hall. We headed into the dorm with no real attempt at stealth. When we got in unmolested, I called out in Arabic to the Djinn Alonia Nazeed. I commanded her to appear before me. After a few tension seconds, we realized she wasn’t actually home. So much for my occult abilities. We decided to use the time to cut off Sainsbury Hall from Adolphe and deal with the eight bodies, effectively sealing Alonia off, all at once. There was nothing we knew we could do to force the Outsider to manifest back into the material world so that was the best option we had. While Snowflake handed off Boneyard duties to Nightshade so he could go get the crest he made, Rose and I went looking for trouble in the upper floors. She led the way back up to a secret chamber and moved with determination to a very nice desk set into the wall. When she opened the top drawer, a cloth doll sprang out at her face with a ragged hanger. I had no idea what was happening but she apparently expected it and snatched the animated rag out of the air without flinching. She started scolding it like a kid that really screws something up when they know better. She had claws but for some reason didn’t just rip its head off and step on the mangled knot of a body as it hit the floor. She claimed that it didn’t want to fight us but Adolphe’s control was too strong for it to disobey. I have no idea how she got all that from a rag doll, waving a hanger like a knife, with buttons for eyes and a stitch for a mouth, but either way I wasn’t buying it. It was still trying to get loose and she wouldn’t let me just rip it to shreds and be done with it.

                                                            About that time, we got a call over the ear curl that Ronin’s delusional cop friend, Tart just arrived on the scene. I didn’t understand until Snowflake described the “special armor” Tart was wearing. I’ve heard Tart ramble about Wookalars before. I don’t know him like Ronin so I can’t really say he’s nuts, but I suspect. His partner was killed by something...though no one knows what. I suspect if he really isn’t schizophrenic, that something supernatural did kill his partner but altered his memories just enough to let his paranoia fill in the blanks. Either way, he had an unlicensed and totally unstable homemade flamethrower strapped to his back and some pieced together bits of “armor.” It’s great that at least one cop believes in the supernatural, but this old guy was in way over his head. We needed to get him and the other two cops still hiding in their patrol car from Mongler, out of there. Snowflake and Ronin discussed what has to be the most cartoon-like idea we’ve ever had to get rid of all the normal people. That sounds so condescending. Maybe we should call them Neverdead. That at least makes them sound cooler. Anyway, so Snowflake made an illusion for all the cops present after we consulted Tavia on what a Wookalar is supposed to look like. As I understand it, a 20 foot tall, boar-like humanoid came out to face Tart, Godzilla-style. There was some broken English taunting about the smell of a fallen meal, I don’t know. Whatever Snowflake did enraged Tart and left the other cops pissing themselves in their cars. I could hear the flamethrower going off and Tart screaming. It was briefly like being back in Afghanistan. That sound in his battle cry. He was ready and willing to die avenging his fallen brother. Suddenly this was much more serious and a whole lot less funny. I told Snowflake to make sure he earned the kill. There was no way this man, this warrior could be denied true vindication. I’m not sure if the Dog was getting into the scene of battle or if the Primeval instincts and memories of war were getting in the way, but thinking about it now, this seems really odd.

                                                            Sadeeuk, your honor could not allow this deception to be another humiliation to one who knew true service and loss. There is nothing odd in this.

                                                            Well at least I can feel better about that now. It’s interesting how the Dog has started to understand my motivations before I do. Anyway, after the vanquished Wookalar dissolved into some kind of black ash and sank into the ground, Tart, along with the other officers left campus secure in the belief that one of their number had saved the day. Then Snowflake started bringing his stuff into Sainsbury Hall to lay claim to it and chop off one more tendril of Adolphe’s power.

                                                            Not long after he got inside, Nightshade came over the ear curl sounding really strange. Apparently after Snowflake went back inside the last time, something tried to get into her head. She fought it off, but she didn’t know if she could do it many more times. I called for Snowflake to go out and get her so she wasn’t alone. Rose and I reverted back to normal form, as nothing was happening and the power can only be sustained so long without expending ourselves more than we already had. Rose locked the doll in Snowflake’s trunk about the time Tavia finally called back. We never did find out what this damn thing was called, but she found enough about it to tell us what we needed. It wasn’t as powerful in sunlight, but that didn’t mean it was weak. If the whole place was blessed, it would be forced to manifest, which Ronin could handle. It could possess people, which Nightshade could attest, so we immediately instituted the buddy rule. We all just kind of looked around when Tavia said it could absorb people whole. While it would eventually dissolve them, they would drown in the abyssal void within before that could happen. It was pretty much immune to anything that didn’t do some kind of chemical or elemental damage. That left me pretty much high and dry, so I took Nightshade with me and declared I was going to find the Science building to make “Something (TM),” while Ronin (with a hidden Rose) went about blessing the campus. Once we found the damn Chem Lab, I mixed up some test tubes with slivers of Phosphorus in kerosene with a bit of added sulfur compounds, just to make things a little less stable. I didn’t want to take the time in a strange lab to try and do something elaborate anyway. I managed to safely put together six vials of what I hoped would be a chemical explosive. It’s always hard to tell what will count when you are dealing with things from outside the known universe. Yeah it’s weird to read and write that.

                                                            While we were gone, the rest of the Krewe figured out that the Outsider could only possess the campus proper, nothing past the recognized boundaries. So we worked it out that Mongler, Cupcake, and I would leave campus, then circle around so we could rush the area where Ronin finished the mass blessing. Rose was of course still in hiding nearby so we could be able to get to Ronin quickly. At least that was the plan. Cupcake and I powered up (Monstered-Out, if you will), and got ready for a big fight. Mongler is apparently always ready but we can’t see it. When Ronin completed the last of the rites, we heard … well nothing at first. Ronin apparently flung her silent daggers of Rage into the beast as soon as it appeared but then it just swallowed her before we even realized the fight started. Rose called us in and joined the fray. Nightshade was doing something with Boneyard as I know Snowflake doesn’t call the lightning. We moved knowing that Ronin was already losing air and Rose was there alone against the Outsider. Mongler said something about the heart of an antlion as soon as we got in sight of the mobile darkness as another bolt of lightning stabbed the darkness from above. Rose tried to get the beast to attack her as I could feel the cold winds blowing from her flowing veils. Amusingly, the darkness still failed to strike her. I hurled one of my vials of Greek Fire at the blob. It exploded, but I couldn’t really tell how well it worked. Lightning arced down once again before Rose chilled the creature with her frozen touch. I quickly realized Cupcake had nothing she could do and gave her three of my vials. She put one to good use as well, and the explosion definitely had some effect. But before we could press our attack, the blackness disappeared with Ronin standing still. I was confused as we couldn’t have killed it that easily, but then my heart sank. Ronin opened her eyes and they shone with a luminescent blackness. No I don’t know how to say it otherwise. Black light was coming out of her eyes. I ran up and swung, hoping to knock her out. It was all I could think of as I wasn’t about to use my claws. Terrifyingly, as I struck my Krewemate as a seven foot dog-man full on the chin, she turned her head to the side from the blow, then looked back in anger at me. Before I could consider all the possible ways I could die, Mongler leveled a massive blow that took her off her feet and planted her flatly on her back. We knew that if we could do enough damage, The Angel could allow Ronin to pass out. And thankfully, It seemed to recognize this as well. We gathered a mostly safe distance around Ronin’s fallen form, when a churning, inky cloud of blackness poured out of her mouth. Rose slapped it with her frigid strike while Cupcake both threw dud vials. Then, like some final scene from a horror movie, Ronin’s eyes flew open and she threw her invisible Rage into the cloud. I threw my last vial of Greek Fire (which did explode) just before another crackling bolt of icy blue lightning crashed into the blob. Then it seemed to fall almost in slow motion, then dispersed into nothing, and was gone.

                                                            We gathered Ronin and moved back to Sainsbury where I could setup a temporary infirmary. I convinced Nightshade to come upstairs so I could try and heal some of her wounds. While we were upstairs, we heard through the ear curl that the Chief of Police was downstairs and “interviewing” Ronin. From what we could hear, that went much better than it could have. The Chief seemed to know about my nocturnal activities and a bit about our otherwise unknown reputation as paranormal investigators. He already had a report about the Wookalar and while he suspected Officer Tart would be sanctioned from those up the chain for abandoning his post while on duty, little of any lasting damage would happen to him. Surprisingly, the only real snag came when Ronin tried to explain we still needed to take one of the eight bodies that Nightshade at some point raised to the front lawn. Snowflake stepped in at that point and … well I’m not sure what he did. I’ll simplify it by saying he put the whammy on the Chief. Suddenly one of the eight bodies was accepted to be the child’s skeleton upstairs. Snowflake placed another illusion over the area so we could grab DeCastro’s skeleton. We took it back to Dark Haven Bluffs while Snowflake and Rose remained at Sainsbury Hall. I heard Mongler tell the Chief they had permission from school officials as we were leaving. I figure Snowflake will have laid claim to the Hall by morning and that will be one less anchor for Adolphe.

                                                            But that’s enough fighting for one night. I know that Nightshade is stable and I’ll be able to keep working with her so she heals faster. For now though, I’m going to fall down myself.


                                                            Jasmine’s Journal

                                                            Saturday, July 24, 2010, Late Night

                                                            It’s been an exhausting day, but all’s well that ends well, as they say.

                                                            We all hurried to Albertus Magnus to stop DeCastro’s Outsider beast from rampaging about. I was with Gwen and Alexia, and we arrived first, thanks to Gwen’s reckless driving. Simon drove Doc and Natalie separately and arrived a bit after we did.

                                                            After arriving, Gwen, Alexia and I began prowling the campus, looking for any signs of the amorphous beast’s presence. But we found nothing except absolute stillness. There were no birds to be seen and even the insects were quiet.

                                                            I’d already called Mongler via Gretchen, so I knew he was on his way. And Gwen called in a favor from her friend Clevon Norris, who got the police to blockade the campus under some story that there was a shooter or serial killer or something there. We didn’t want anyone getting hurt, so the fewer people on campus, the better. Of course, when Mongler did arrive, the police were trying to stop him, but he frightened them away with a big, toothy grin. Since I doubt they could see what he really looks like, I must assume he used the fear powers of his kind to scare them off.

                                                            The next few hours were a bit chaotic. When we arrived, I unlocked my Primeval nature and allowed the Caul and Shroud to come over me, adopting my feline features and growing vicious, spectral claws. Doc remained with Natalie in Simon’s car in a trance, projecting his consciousness clad in the guise of the Dog of War to fight whatever we might encounter. Alexia grew additional, knife-wielding arms, and Simon sank into a Boneyard trance to search the campus for our adversary. Gwen was ready to go in guns blazing.

                                                            But Simon discovered that the beast was not manifested. It had possessed the campus, and seemed to lurking in that state, waiting. We had no idea how to draw it out, and I think Doc and I, locked in our Primeval states like we were, felt the restless pull of the predator’s instinct. I know I, for one, was a bit frustrated that we couldn’t simply face the beast. I’m sure it affected my later behavior.

                                                            By the time Mongler joined us, we were looking for other ways to confront the beast, and Gwen had called Tavia to have her research the thing, hoping she could dig up enough information to help us. While we were waiting on that, we decided to go investigate Sansbury Hall. With DeCastro gone, we could unearth the corpses beneath the place and destroy DeCastro’s body. Furthermore, we though Alonia might know something that would help us, if we could force her to share.

                                                            But Alonia was not at the hall. She had no doubt fled to her other anchor, which left Sansbury Hall at our disposal. The ritual chambers in the basement did not hold any answers, so I marched right upstairs and into the secret room where I had found the doll before. I opened the drawer and the beastly thing jumped at me! But I was faster and snatched it out of the air, holding it quite securely in one of my clawed hands as I gave it a piece of my mind! I told it we were tired of its mischief and that it could either settle down and help us free it from its master’s iron grip or we could do things the hard way. After all, the spirit possessing the doll isn’t inherently evil, it’s just been forced to serve DeCastro. After I made our intentions clear, its struggles quieted somewhat. It was still trying to attack me, for it had no choice under DeCastro’s control, but its efforts became almost lethargic. Once this is all over, hopefully we’ll be able to free the poor thing, or at least give it a better home.

                                                            I don’t think Doc trusted the doll. He didn’t seem convinced that it wasn’t still intent on killing me, and continued to threaten it for the next bit while I carried the thing around to keep it out of trouble. Unfortunately, it didn’t seem to know anything that would help us.

                                                            Meanwhile, some of the police, including a certain Officer Tart, began preparing to enter the campus to search for whatever was causing the disturbance. I don’t really understand exactly what the situation was, but apparently the officer in question believes in the supernatural and thought some sort of beast that killed his late partner was lurking about. We couldn’t have him getting involved, so Simon manufactured an illusion of this imaginary beast and let the officer have it out, ultimately slaying the thing. Simon them made the remains dissolve. Officer Tart seemed placated, though I’m certain his superiors are unhappy. Still, several of his fellow officers witnessed the whole thing, so hopefully he won’t be committed or fired.

                                                            With that crisis averted, Simon hurried home to get his family crest so that we could establish our dominion over Sansbury Hall while Alonia and Adolphe were out. That way, Adolphe would no longer be able to use it as an anchor, and if we could destroy Adolphe’s body, then Sansbury Hall would also be safe from Alonia. Natalie, still lying in the car, took Simon’s place monitoring things through the Boneyard, protected by Alexia.

                                                            It took Simon awhile to place the crest and perform the rituals necessary to claim Sansbury Hall, and while he was busy with that, Doc and I continued to prowl restlessly, waiting for word from Tavia. I locked the doll in the trunk of Simon’s car (it seemed to understand why I had to do it). Natalie used her Boneyard mastery of Grave Dirt to unearth all the bodies beneath Sansbury Hall, shifting them onto the grounds outside, where Gwen’s oracular insight easily let her determine which belonged to Adolphe. Meanwhile, I crept into the basement and found the spot where the Sansbury Hall’s plasm collects. I absorbed in the plasm and then marked the place with our krewe mark and our sign for “haunt.”

                                                            We had a close call, though, as Natalie was very nearly possessed by the thing through her Boneyard. She managed to fend it off, but the experience seemed to sober her up a bit, as she had been quite loopy from blood loss (and I suspect she was exaggerating her behavior).

                                                            Eventually, Tavia called back information on the beast. Here’s a bit of what Tavia learned:

                                                            In order to force it manifest, we would need to bless the entire campus. Then it would mostly manifest near someone who it perceived as alone and vulnerable.

                                                            It can possess people.

                                                            In its physical form, it is an amorphous creature that cannot be harmed by most physical attacks. Only spiritual attacks or attacks that cause a substantial chemical reaction, such as burning, electricity, or freezing would harm it.

                                                            It can envelop people in its shadowy mass, imprisoning them there so that they suffocate. Eventually, those trapped inside

                                                            it would be dissolved, but the process would take a long time, and a victim would be dead from suffocation long before they were digested.

                                                            We had also figured out that the beast’s possession and awareness only extended to the boundaries of campus, so we decided that Gwen would set about blessing the grounds to draw the beast out. To ensure that it would come for one of us, Gwen would seem to be alone, though in truth, I would be hidden invisibly with her to help defend her from the beast once it appeared. The others would wait just outside of campus to avoid the beast’s notice, but close to the final spot Gwen would bless so that they could rush in to help us as quickly as possible.

                                                            Also, Doc realized that he and Alexia had no real way to harm the beast, so he went to the science department’s chemistry lab and concocted a few flasks of incendiary chemicals. Doc really is an ingenious and versatile man when he puts his mind to it.

                                                            The plan worked, though I can’t say it went smoothly. As Gwen completed the Catholic blessing, the beast appeared like a pool of shadow pouring from thin air, its gaping mouth and hideous eyes staring ravenously at Gwen. She immediately attacked it with the force of her Rage, while I shrugged off my Stillness Shroud and donned the Cold-Wind. Unfortunately, I was not quick enough, and the beast enveloped Gwen. She’s a tough one, though, so it didn’t stop her from tearing at it with her Rage until the others arrived. Doc and Alexia came in tossing the incendiaries at it, while Natalie expended virtually all of her spiritual strength blasting it with arcs of electricity through her Cold-Wind Boneyard. Simon remained out of the fight, protecting Natalie’s body.

                                                            The beast tried to attack me, and I was willing to let it envelop me since I was cloaked in my Cold-Wind Shroud and knew I could freeze it from within. But it seemed to sense the danger and recoiled from me. Then it possessed Gwen. She turned against us. Doc and Mongler wasted no time knocking her out of action with a couple of well-placed punches. With its host unconscious, the beast was forced to manifest again. I froze bits of it, and then Natalie killed it once and for all with a final strike of electricity.

                                                            The rest was just clean-up. By this time, the police chief had shown up, and the police were moving onto campus and establishing a crime scene. Gwen managed to smooth it over, and I think we have some clout now as paranormal investigators who really know what we’re doing. The chief was not happy, but whether or not he actually understands what happened tonight, he trusts Gwen and was generous enough to conspire with us to remove DeCastro’s body before the police took it so that we can destroy it as soon as possible. Actually, I’m not so so certain that Simon didn’t use some of his tricks to hide DeCastro’s corpse and convince the chief that it was all for the best, but in any event, we were able to steal the body without any trouble from the police.

                                                            While the others removed DeCastro’s corpse to Darkhaven Bluffs, Simon and I remained on campus and made a quick stop by Murder Ballads to absorb the plasm that had managed to collect there since we last did so. We were both quite drained, and in fact I had nothing left. It felt good to strengthen my chi with the infusion of spiritual energy, especially after such an exhausting few days.

                                                            Then we made our way home and incinerated the body in the incinerator that’s built into one of the garden mausoleums. I don’t really want to know why Simon’s family has a corpse-sized incinerator in the garden, but I will admit that it could be handy for situations like this.

                                                            Now we just have to go deal with Alonia and Adolphe, which will be a challenge, but should be much easier than it might have been had we dealt with them together as Sansbury Hall.

                                                            For now, though, I’m going to get some much deserved rest.


                                                            Footnotes

                                                            John McClane
                                                            I don’t recall exactly how it came up, but somehow as we were hunting for DeCastro’s beast on campus, Gwen said something to the effect that she was ready to “Go all John McClane on the thing.” I misunderstood and after a moment of puzzling over it, asked exactly how Republican politics would help us. Gwen looked at me like I was crazy for few seconds until she realized my error and explained that she meant John McClane, a character from an action movie called Die Hard, not John McCain, the presidential candidate.

                                                            End Session L

                                                            Roman Numerals are cool till you get to session L.

                                                            So they met their first Abyssal Entity face to face, a Gulmoth. Fortunately, it could not stand against the combined efforts of the entire Krewe, and they managed to kill it. Probably.

                                                            FYI Grin (Mongler) used Fleeting Autumn on the Gulmoth so it would not run away (a worst case scenario) by removing it's ability to feel fear. He similarly used Fleeting Autumn on the Police. The creature generally tried to possess people who were alone. It could possess an area as marked by peoples understanding of the boarders...ie the campus for example.

                                                            They also have drawn more police scrutiny, which may become a problem long term. In the meantime, they managed to, uh, give Timothy Tart a chance to revenge his fallen partner on an evil Wookalar, at least in his head. That encounter was…no pre-planned…I mean when Officer Tart lost his shit and went and got his Wookalar killing gear I knew how that would work but I did not pre-plan it for this session. I have to say it was not conducive to a horror mood but once it started it was it’s own beast. I got a private suggestion that the final battle for the Krewe should be against a Wookalar. We will see. I have not even decided if they exist or not.

                                                            Also Jasmine called a possessed doll a wanker.

                                                            Next session was almost entirely Rping the following day. Join us for when Simon and Doc have a serious talk about women, Alexia, Jasmine and Gwen do girl stuff, and Natalie expresses a desire for her own Dreamy Boom Boom.
